It then sets bits 11:10 of the region word at +0x88 to 1 and sends it with 0x10405; sets the halfword +0x6a to 1; picks the draw-buffer word (the value above, forced to 0xc for class 7) and, when it differs from the cached word at region +0x2b68, stores it and sends it with 0x10424 between the settle forms with 0x10019 and the 0x11017 descriptor words from region +0x1b24 to +0x1b30 (as held, with the third word 0, with bit 23 of the third word set and as held once more); sends 0x10422 with the draw-control word (the raster-op, the depth-derived bits, bit 19 for classes 0, 1 and 7 and the mask bits of the window's entry, stored at +0xc8); rewrites +0x1b24 and +0x1b2c from the entry's words and resends the descriptor and the 0x11453 words (+0x1b34 to +0x1b44, the first from the entry's +0x14) after settle forms; saves the region word at +0x2680 into +0x1b7c and replaces it with 0x12f (sent as the register-select 0x29008000 form); sends 0x10658 and 0x120000; and stores the four rectangle corners as floats at +0x1b5c to +0x1b6c (x1, y1 at +0x1b5c and +0x1b60, x2 at +0x1b68, y2 at +0x1b6c, zeros between) and sends 0x4b0 with the entry's word at +0x18 ORed with 0x13c00 (stored at +0x1b8c).
  The window class then indexes a 12-entry jump table (0x172ac00): classes 0, 1 and 7 share one case, 2 to 6 and 8 to 11 have their own; a class of 12 or more prints "odsyDrawImageDMA: Unrecognized mode: 0x%x" (the message at rodata +0x1a0). Every case sets bit 20 of the region word at +0x84 (not in the case for classes 0, 1 and 7, where the word is the draw-buffer value ORed with the buffer index) and sends it with 0x10404; then sends the program-select pair 0x22008001 and 0x23008003 (cases for classes 0, 1, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11; cached values +0x2664 = 1 and +0x2668 = 3, resent only when either differs) or 0x22008000 and 0x23008002 (classes 2 to 6; cached 0 and 2); and a constant block that depends on the case: 0xce80cc08, three zeros, 0xc580c406 and a zero (classes 0, 1, 7, 8, 9, 10, 11), 0xc580cc09 (classes 5 and 6) or 0xc580cc08 (classes 2, 3, 4) followed by zeros (the exact padding counts were not tallied). It then repacks the plane mask into the words at region +0xe8 and +0xec (cached; resent under 0x11023 with the four words +0xe8 to +0xf4 only when either changed): classes 0, 1, 7, 10 and 11 store the mask shifted left 12 and the value 1; classes 8 and 9 the mask shifted left 16 and 1; class 6 the ten-bit components from bits 9:0, 19:10 and 29:20 spread to the three fields with a 0xfff limit word; class 5 the same with limit 0xc00; class 4 the eight-bit components (bits 7:0, 15:8, 23:16) with limit 0xff0; class 3 five-bit components with limit 0x800; class 2 four-bit components with limit 0xf00. Cases for classes 0, 1 and 7 set the flag `t1` to 1 (read above as bit 19 of the draw-control word), the others 0.
  Both paths end at the same transfer: an 80-byte `ioctl(descriptor, 0x6f640006, block)` at 0x16a9310 (failure "odsyDrawImageDMA: ODSY_MM_XFR pixel transfer failed: '%s'"). The block has the same layout as in `odsyReadImage` (group -1 and -2 at +0 and +4, the buffer address as a 64-bit word at +16, the length, the first header word, the rectangle origin at +32, extent at +40, stride minus row length at +48, row length in the low 20 bits of +52) with the flag word at +60 equal to 3 on the direct path (write, wait) and 0xb on the other (write, wait, unclipped path), and the top byte of the +56 word 0x32 on the direct path and 0x3a on the other. After the transfer on the second path it restores the saved +0x2680 word, zeroes the +0x1b34 word, sends the settle form and the 0x11453 words again, clears bits 11:10 of +0x88, sends 0x10405 again, and sets the flags +0x68 and +0x6a of the board-private record to 1 and 1.

## Start-up state routine

- `odsyInitDefaultHW..N` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15c6610, 5432 bytes): **established** (the whole listing, 0x15c6610 to 0x15c7b44, was read next to the decompilation; the text below corrects the earlier reading where it differed).
  Puts the board and the module's shadow of it into a known starting state when a screen comes up (argument: the screen; no result). It works on the per-board state region (the 0x2e80-byte block of `odsyKernInit`, found through the board-private record's word at +0x5c and the module's table of regions) and pushes words through the board-private record's FIFO pointer (word 0 of the record, stored through after every word). Between most groups it sends the settle sequence of `docs/opengl.md` when the region's pending flag at +0x1cb8 is set (clearing it), and in a few places sends it with no test: 0x10443 with operand 250 (0xfa) then two 0x10046 words, or the longer form 0x10443, operand 150 (0x96), four 0x10046 words.
  It first zeroes the region's first 0x2b78 bytes with `bzero` and loads the shadow words at +0x265c to +0x26b0 (22 words, the selectors of registers 0x20 to 0x35: 3, 0x10, 0, 2, 0xc, 0xe, then 0xd6 and 0xe0 at +0x2680 and +0x2684, 0xea at +0x268c, 0x380 at +0x2694, the rest 0); it sends 19 of them, skipping registers 0x26, 0x2b and 0x2d, each as `(value & 0x3ff) | register << 24 | 0x20000000 | (value >> 10) << 23 | 0x8000` (two registers a pass; the words for registers 0x22 and 0x23 equal the 0x22008000 and 0x23008002 that `odsyDrawImageDMA..H` resends). It then loads parameter-memory constants and tables as `docs/buzz.md` describes for register 0x31 and register 0x34: register 0x31 gets 0x1e0 (word 0x310081e0), then block word 0x9080bda2 with 16 words from region +0x243c: 1.0, 0.5, -1.0, 0, a word computed from a call to `sqrt` with 8.0, 3.0, one from `sqrt` with 2.0, one from a second `sqrt` with 2.0 times 0.25 (stored at +0x2458), then zeros. **The listing converts the integer register v0, not the double result in f0, after each of those three calls** (`mtc1 v0` with `cvt.s.W` at 0x15c67e8, 0x15c680c and 0x15c6844), as if `sqrt` had been declared to return an int. If the shipped `sqrt` leaves v0 untouched (its code was not read here: **unknown**), v0 still holds the 0x20000000 loaded at 0x15c66d8 and the three words are 536870912.0, 536870912.0 and 134217728.0 instead of 2.83, 1.41 and 0.35. **Read in the next passage.** Whether any microcode program reads those three constants is **unknown**: the program table of `ucode/buzz_routines.md` was not searched for readers of parameter-memory words 0x243c to 0x2458 of that register block. Register 0x34 is set to index 0 (word 0x34008000) and gets block word 0x9080bdc8 with a 16-word matrix from region +0x1a10 (rows 1, 0, 0, 0.5; 0, 1, 0, 0.5; 0, 0, 1, 0; 0, 0, 0, 1); it is set to index 0x10 (0x34008010) and gets block word 0x908091df with the 4 words (0, 0, 0, 1.0) from +0x1a50, and is left at index 0. The shadow word at +0x26a0 (register 0x31) is set to 0x141 later, without a send.
  The state words that follow, each a header and operand words, in the order sent: 0x10845 (two words from the board record +0x38 and +0x3c); 0x10452 (the value 0x1ffffff stored at +0xe4); 0x10841 and, after two settle forms, 0x10019, the longer settle and 0x10840 (the zeroed pairs at +0x2b60 and +0x2b6c); 0x11c16 (7 words from +0x14c to +0x164 after the field at bits 25:23 of +0x15c is set to 6); 0x1141b (5 words from +0x138 to +0x148 after the low three bits of +0x138 are set to 1); 0x10405 (the word +0x88 with bits 15:13 set to 1 and bits 11:10 cleared); 0x13c38 (16 words from +0x1070 to +0x10ac, after +0x106c is set to 2); 0x1203b (8 words from +0x1174 to +0x1190, after sixteen words at every second address from +0x1174 to +0x11ec are set to 0x1000 and +0x1170 to 2); 0x10415 (the word +0x8c, set to 10); 0x1084a (+0xdc and +0xe0, both set to 0x80); 0x10424 (+0x2b68, set to 3); 0x10451 (+0x1b20, set to 1); then the 0x11017 descriptor words, sent four times in the order as held (four words from +0x1b24 to +0x1b30, the first two copied from the board record's words +4 and +0xc), with the third word 0, with bit 23 of the third word set, and as held again, separated by settle forms and by a bare 0x20000000 word. It also sets +0xf4 to 0xff and changes the word +0xb4 (bits 15:8 set, bits 21:16 cleared) in the shadow without sending them. The last pushes are the window words: the board record's clip box halfwords at +0x60 to +0x66 become (0, 0, width, height) from the screen's halfwords at +8 and +10, and the clip-limit halfwords at +0x68 to +0x72 are set to (0, 1, 0xfff, 0xfff, 0, 0) and then, as the first test finds them not already equal to the screen size, to the screen size; the region +0x2b40 gets the origin word (the box's y in the low 12 bits, its x above) and +0x2b44 the extent word (height minus 1 in the low 12 bits, width minus 1 above), the six words from +0x2b48 alternate 0xffffff and 0, and all eight go out under header 0x1204b; then 0x1044c with the word +0x1cac (cleared to 0).
  It stores the current colour-map generation (the halfword at index +0x2b78 of the table at +0x2b7c) into the halfword at +0x2b3c, registers `odsyBlockHandler..L` and `odsyWakeupHandler..M` with `RegisterBlockAndWakeupHandlers`, and clears the planes. A region is built from the clip box with the screen's region-initialise procedure (screen +0x154, **inferred** from its argument shape) and `odsyClearPlanes` is called with it, once with value 0 and once with 1000 (0x3e8). When the structure behind screen +0x74 has a head layout word at +0xdc of 1 or more, the region is first intersected (screen +0x164) with a rectangle built from the halfwords at +0xe0 and +0xe2 of that structure (layout 1: from y = the +0xe2 value to the sum of +0xe0 and +0xe2, the full width; other layouts: from 0 to +0xe0), cleared, translated by the +0xe2 value (screen +0x178) and cleared again, each time with both values; the region is then freed (screen +0x160). Finally it calls `odsyDisplayInit` and `odsyCursorInit` and enables the video timing generator with `ioctl(descriptor, 0x6f640020, {1})`, printing "odsyEnableVTG: ODSY_BRDMGR_VTG_CONTROL Failed '%s'" with `strerror(errno)` on a failure. What each state word selects on the board beyond the names in `docs/words.md` is **unknown**.

## Video format loading routines

- `odsyLoadAndFree` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16ffbe0, 1400 bytes): **established.**
  Applies a chosen video format to one head and releases the format list that selected it (arguments: screen, head number, the format-information array to free, a word passed through, the chosen format record with its name at +0, a load flag; result 0 on success, 2 when the load or the monitor enable failed). The routine is stored in the video-control function table by `odsyAttachXvc`, so its callers are in the Xvc layer. The load flag is the sixth argument. When it is 0 the routine only records the choice: it frees the array, builds the path "/var/X11/Xvc/Odyssey%d_TimingTable" from the screen's number, reads that file with `odsyReadTTfilename` (a result of 3 prints "unable to read or create file"), rewrites it with `odsyWriteTTfilename` using the new format name and the two integers that read returned (a failure prints "unable to write"), and returns 0 without touching the board. This answers the question left open in `odsyLdFmtByName..OC` about the file-mode register: the branch is selected by the sixth argument being zero, and the same shape appears in both routines. With the flag set and no compositor mode (the board record's word at +0x1cc zero, or bits 2:1 of it nonzero, or the head's entry word at +0x1e8 differing from it) it copies the format name, appends ".vfo", builds the database path under "/usr/gfx/ucode/ODSY/vof" with `odsyMakeFileName`, opens it with `SDBreadOpenDatabase` ("Failed to open", result 2) and loads the timing with `odsyLoadTimingTable..CC` using the video-control record's word at +0x18 ("odsyLoadTimingTable Failed", result 2). If the screen is the current one it then calls `odsyCursorOn(1, screen)`. For head 0 and a format whose name differs from the head entry's current one (strcmp against entry +0x14), it frees the entry's old field array at +0x144, copies the 0x138-byte format record into the entry at +0x14, allocates a field array of 0x24-byte records (count at record +300, "Couldn't allocate field info" and result 2 on failure), copies the fields from record +0x130, sets the entry's flag word at +0 to 1 and stores the width and height as floats: 0 at +4 and +8, the record's +0x100 at +0xc and +0x104 at +0x10. When the video-control record's word at +4 is 2 (two heads) the same format is copied to the second head's entry at +0x1f8 + 0x14, whose flag at +0x1f8 is set to 1 and whose words at +0x1fc, +0x200, +0x204 and +0x208 are the record's +0x104 as a float, 0, the record's +0x100 as a float and its +0x104 again; the cursor routines compare the pointer position with the word at +0x1fc to pick the head, so the second head starts at x equal to that value; `odsyAttachXvc` stores the screen width in the first entry's word at +0x10 and half of it as the second entry's word at +4 (dual head), which fixes the entry layout as +4 x origin, +8 y origin, +0xc height and +0x10 width, all floats, and makes the record's +0x104 the head width (**inferred** from that match). In the compositor case it calls `odsyLoadCompositorTiming..LC` instead. All paths that reach the end set `screenIsSaved` to 1 and call `odsyBlankScreen(0, screen)` ("Cannot Enable Monitor" when it fails), free the array and close the database.
- `odsyDefaultTTExists` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16962a0, 4020 bytes): **established** (the whole listing, 0x16962a0 to 0x1697250, was read next to the decompilation; the floating-point compares were checked with their condition-code operands in a separate disassembly, because the decompilation drops one of them).
  Finds a timing-table format that the board and monitor can start with, and returns its geometry (arguments: screen, an output buffer for the file name, six output words for pixel width, screen height, frame-buffer width, frame-buffer height and the depth-table index, then a last argument handed to the file reader; result 1 when a usable file was found, 10 for an unknown depth, 0 when a needed value is missing). It reads the "/var/X11/Xvc/Odyssey%d_TimingTable" file with `odsyReadTTfilename` (a result of 1 or 3 prints "unable to read or create file" and marks the search as using the default), appends ".vfo" to a name that does not start with '/', and looks the depth the file gave up in the four-entry table at `odsyScrnPriv` + 0x4428, storing the index through the sixth output word; no match prints "%d is not a valid framebuffer depth" and returns 10.
  It then loops over candidate files. A name starting with '/' is used as a path (a trailing newline is cut); otherwise the path is the environment variable `GL_GRROOT`, then "/usr/gfx/ucode/ODSY/vof", a '/' when missing, then the name. If the database does not open, the routine is fatal when it is already on the default ("default timing file '%s' not found") and otherwise prints the errno, asks `odsyValidateBoardConfig` for a board-appropriate default name, appends ".vfo" and repeats. From the database it reads with `SDBgetSymbolValueScalar` (a miss prints "odsyGetScalar: unable to find %s", ends the routine and clears the name buffer, result 0): AchievedFrequency (a double), FormatInfo_Interlaced, FormatInfo_DualHead, FormatInfo_DigitalSkew (the only one whose miss is ignored; it then stays 0), FormatInfo_FullScreenStereo, FormatInfo_Stereo, TotalPixelsPerLine, DCDTotalPixelsPerLine, DCDSupport, DCDHorizontalBackPorch, DCDHorizontalFrontPorch, DCDHorizontalSync (their sum is kept), ActivePixelsPerLine, ActiveLinesPerFrame and FormatInfo_FieldSequential. Stereo is folded into one word: 0 for none, 1 for stereo, 4 when full-screen stereo is also set, plus 2 for field-sequential.
  A format is refused by the rules below. A refusal prints "Illegal timing file '%s' specified" and the default name is substituted before the loop repeats, and is fatal ("Illegal default timing file '%s'") when the default itself was refused. On a board whose record halfword at +0x3a is 3: DCDSupport must be nonzero; AchievedFrequency must lie from 60.0 to 180.0 MHz, or, when the module word at 0x173aa10 (set by platform parameter 0xc) is nonzero, must not exceed 220.0 (no lower limit then); the format must be neither interlaced, nor full-screen stereo, nor field-sequential; and when that word is zero and there is no dual head and no digital skew, the product of 500.0 and the ratio TotalPixelsPerLine over DCDTotalPixelsPerLine, truncated, must not be below the DCD blanking sum. A 1600 by 1024 format is always refused when the first monitor of the board record (valid halfword +0xc6) has the identity bytes 4c f8 41 06 at +0xd0 to +0xd3, and when the second (valid halfword +0x148) has them at +0x152 to +0x155: every outcome of the match tests ends in a refusal, so the rule means "this monitor does not take 1600 by 1024" (what that monitor is, and why, is **unknown**: no software or published source read so far names it). On other boards a dual-head format or one with a digital skew is refused. On any board whose words at +0x28 and +0x18 are 0 and 2, a frequency above 109.0 and below 194.0 is also refused, and so is exactly 108.0 or exactly 107.2512 when the board-private word +0x10 is 3. VfoVersion must be nonzero and FormatInfo_FullScreenStereo must be zero ("Illegal format for X server startup"). On that board type with depth code 0x10 and a name beginning "1280x1024_60" the routine prints that 16 bits a pixel is unsupported with that table (and tells the user to pick new defaults in the toolchest) and sets the depth index to 2.
  On success it reads ActivePixelsPerLine, ScreenHeight, FrameBufferHeight, FrameBufferWidth and FormatInfo_DualHead, rounds the frame-buffer height up to a multiple of 16 and the width up to a multiple of 32, and stores them: with dual head on a halfword-3 board the frame-buffer width is doubled, the pixel width is twice ActivePixelsPerLine and halfword +0x3e of the board record becomes 1, otherwise they are as read and +0x3e becomes 0. The board-information record from `ddxGetBoardInfo` gets the width at +0x20 and the height at +0x22, the screen height goes to the second output, and the routine returns 1 with the database closed. What the halfword at +0x3a of the board record (value 3 for the first rule set) names is **unknown**.

## Gamma ramp routines

The gamma ramp the module keeps for a screen sits behind the video-control record: the word at +0x24 points to a record whose first word is the sample count (0x1000), whose second is 10, and whose word at +0xc points to three tables of 0x600 16-bit entries, red at +0, green at +0xc00 and blue at +0x1800. Entry n of a table stands for a range of the 4096 input samples: entries 0 to 0x1ff are samples 0 to 0x1ff, entries 0x200 to 0x2ff are every second sample from 0x200, and entries 0x300 to 0x5ff are every fourth sample from 0x400 (the compressed index that `odsyLoadGamma0` also walks). The board takes the ramp as 0x600 colour-map entries at index 0x1a00 + n, each the three 16-bit values reduced to 10 bits and packed as red 29:20, green 19:10, blue 9:0.

- `odsyStoreGamma16` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x158f900, 872 bytes): **established.**
  Replaces selected channels of the gamma ramp with a 16-bit ramp and sends the result (arguments: screen, channel mask, unused, sample count, ramp pointer). The mask selects red (bit 0), green (bit 1) and blue (bit 2). The sample count must equal the ramp's first word or "the size of the ramp passed doesn't match the gamma size" is printed and nothing changes. It reserves a 0xf8a0-byte stack block as a colour-load request (count 0x1f00, entries at +0x10; a zero from the reservation prints "couldn't allocate memory"). For each of the 4096 samples the first loop uses the compressed index above, so it visits 0x600 samples: the next ramp value is copied into the selected channels' tables at that index (the stores guarded by mask bits 1, 2 and 4) and an entry (index 0x1a00 plus the table index, packed colour from all three tables) is written. It then fills the byte tables of the video-control record at +0xa8, +0x1a8 and +0x2a8 with an identity run 0 to 0xff (the second loop, whose end test is the constant 0xfe), copies the 0x1900 ordinary colour entries from the board record's shadow table (the pointer at +0x54) into the request, and calls `odsyLoadCmap` and then `odsyGammaFit` with the table pointer.
- `odsyStoreGamma8` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16aea90, 872 bytes): **established.**
  The 8-bit form of `odsyStoreGamma16` (same arguments, the ramp is bytes). Each byte b is widened to the 16-bit value b times 0x101 (`CONCAT11` of the byte with itself), so 0xff becomes 0xffff, before it goes into the selected channels' tables; the sample walk, the identity byte tables, the shadow-colour fill, the `odsyLoadCmap` call and the `odsyGammaFit` call are the same, and the failure messages read "odsyStoreGamma8".
- `odsyStoreCompGamma` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x167a3a0, 724 bytes): **established.**
  Sends the stored gamma ramp again without changing it (argument: the screen). It builds the same 0x1f00-entry request as `odsyStoreGamma16`, but every ramp entry comes from the existing three tables and none is rewritten, then fills the identity byte tables and the ordinary colours as there. Around the `odsyLoadCmap` call it sets the board record's word at +0x13c to 1 and puts the old value back afterwards, so the request is always sent to the kernel (`odsyLoadCmap` sends only when that word is 1); then it calls `odsyGammaFit`. That it exists to re-send the ramp after a compositor change is **inferred** from the forced +0x13c value, which `odsyLoadCmap` tests.
- `odsyInitGammaMap..ED` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1592b80, 1396 bytes): **established.**
  Builds the default gamma ramp for a screen (arguments: screen, video-control gamma record; result 1, or 0 when the 0x30c0-byte stack block cannot be reserved: "couldn't allocate memory"). It sets the record's sample count to 0x1000, its second word to 10 and its table pointer to the tables that follow the record header. Each table entry is `(x ^ (1/gammaAdjustVal)) * 65535 + 0.5` truncated to 16 bits, where x is the sample number divided by 4095 (the module multiplies by 16.003663 and divides by 65535.0) and `gammaAdjustVal` is the module's current fitted value; all three channels get the same value. The loops visit the samples in the three ranges of the compressed index, entries 0 to 0x1ff, 0x200 to 0x2ff and 0x300 to 0x5ff, and each also fills one colour-map entry (index 0x1a00 plus the entry number, with the 10-bit value packed three times). It fills the byte identity tables as `odsyStoreGamma16` does, sends the 0x600 entries with `odsyLoadCmap`, and then stores 292 divided by `gammaAdjustVal`, truncated and limited to 255, in the board-private record at +0x38 and the constant 0x76ff at +0x3c; these are the two operand words of the 0x10845 command that `odsyInitDefaultHW..N` sends.
- `odsyQueryGammaColors` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15ae930, 292 bytes): **established.**
  Reads one channel of the gamma ramp back as 4096 samples (arguments: screen, channel 1, 2 or 3 for red, green or blue, output array of 4096 halfwords). The channel picks the table at +0, +0xc00 or +0x1800 behind the video-control record's pointer chain (+0x24 then +0xc); any other channel number leaves the table pointer unset. The loop that compares against 0x200 and 0x400 (0x15ae9d0 and 0x15ae99c) writes two samples per pass, sample i taking the table entry at the compressed index of i (i itself below 0x200, half of it plus 0x100 below 0x400, a quarter of it plus 0x200 above), so an entry of the 0x600-entry table appears once, twice or four times in the output, by the range it belongs to.
- `odsyGammaFit` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16f6730, 536 bytes): **established.**
  Fits a single exponent to the current ramp and sends the matching display setting (arguments: screen, the pointer to the 16-bit tables). For each of the 0x600 table entries it takes y, the mean of the three channel values (the sum divided by 3 through a multiply by 0xaaaaaaab), and x, the sample the entry stands for (j for entries below 0x200, 2j minus 0x200 up to 0x3ff, 4j minus 0x800 above); entries with y of 0 or x not above 0 are skipped. It accumulates the base-10 logarithms (natural logs times 0.4342945): Sx, Sy, the sum of the products and the sum of the squares of log x, with n the number of entries used, and stores the module's `gammaAdjustVal` as (n Sxx minus Sx squared) over (n Sxy minus Sx Sy), the reciprocal of the least-squares slope of log y against log x. It sends the command word 0x10845 to the display FIFO (through the board-private record's pointer at word 0), then 292.0 divided by `gammaAdjustVal` truncated and limited to 255, then the constant 0x76ff. That the 0x10845 operands set a display-side gain from the fitted exponent is **inferred** from the pairing with `odsyInitGammaMap..ED`, which stores the same two values in the record.

## Video-control attach and post-initialisation

- `odsyAttachXvc` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1605fd0, 4348 bytes): **established** (the whole listing, 0x1605fd0 to 0x16070c8, was read next to the decompilation, with the rodata strings resolved at base 0x172acc0).
  Creates the video-control record of a screen and fills its function table (arguments: screen, name of the video-format file to start with; result 1, or 0 after an allocation or file failure). It stores in the module word at 0x173aa0c whether the environment variable `XVC_DEBUG` is set, and on the first screen of the process (a flag word at +0x10 of the module's data block) calls `odsyQueryHyperpipeNetwork` once with the screen number and 0. The head count is 1; it is 2 when the board-private record's halfword at +0x3a is 3 and the halfword at +0x3e is 1; and it is 3 when the board record's word at +0x1cc or +0x1d0 is nonzero (0x1606038 to 0x1606060, 0x16065c8). It allocates 0x27bc plus 0x1f8 bytes per head with `Xalloc` and zeroes them (a null allocation returns 0). The head count goes to +4, the head array (entries of 0x1f8 bytes from +0x3ac) to +8, 3 to +0xc, 1 to +0x10, +0x14 and +0x20, -1 to +0x18, and the block after the last head to +0x24 (the one the gamma routines follow). The function table is at +0x34: +0x34 `odsyQryMonitor`, +0x38 `odsyResetProc`, +0x3c `odsyPostInit`, +0x40 `odsyLstVideoFmt`, +0x44 `odsyLoadAndFree`, +0x48 `odsyLstVideoFmtCombs..BC`, +0x4c `odsyListFormatsInComb..IB`, +0x50 `odsyLdCombination..AD`, +0x58 `odsySetCntrl..ID`, +0x5c `odsyQryCntrl..JD`, +0x68 `odsySetScrnGlock`, +0x6c `odsyGenlockHandler..FD`, +0x70 `odsySetPltParam`, +0x74 `odsyQryPltParam`, +0x78 `odsyStoreGamma8`, +0x7c `odsyStoreGamma16`, +0x80 `odsyQueryGammaColors`, +0x8c `odsySetChannelInputRectangle..IC`, +0x90 `odsyQueryBestChannelRectangle..HC`, +0x94 `odsyMonitorCommand..BD`, +0x98 `odsyInitMonitorProtocol..CD`, +0x9c `odsySetExternalLock..GD`, +0xa0 `odsyQryExtSyncNm..HD` and +0xa4 `odsyLdFmtByName..OC`. The first head gets the screen's height and width as floats at +0x3b8 and +0x3bc, and the 0x138-byte format-name area at +0x3c0 is zeroed.
  The format file is opened as a path when its name starts with '/' (a newline is cut off), otherwise `odsyMakeFileName` builds it under "/usr/gfx/ucode/ODSY/vof"; `SDBreadOpenDatabase` failing prints "failed to open %s sdb file ... specified in /var/X11/Xvc/Odyssey%d_TimingTable file ... using default monitor settings instead" (the %d is a byte of the board record behind the screen's slot) and takes the common failure exit. From the database it reads, each with `SDBgetSymbolValueScalar` and, on a miss, "odsyGetScalar: unable to find %s" and the failure exit: FieldsPerFrame (+0x4ec), FormatInfo_Interlaced, FormatInfo_Stereo, FormatInfo_FullScreenStereo and FormatInfo_FieldSequential (locals), VerticalRetraceRate (a double: the float at +0x4d4 is the rate, the float at +0x4d0 is the rate times FieldsPerFrame when the format is not interlaced and the rate itself when it is), and ScreenHeight (+0x4c0), ActivePixelsPerLine (+0x4c4), TotalLinesPerFrame (+0x4c8), TotalPixelsPerLine (+0x4cc), FormatInfo_PixelClock (+0x4d8), FormatInfo_H_BackPorch (+0x4dc), FormatInfo_H_Sync (+0x4e0), FormatInfo_H_FrontPorch (+0x4e4) and FormatInfo_H_Active (+0x4e8). The stereo word at +0x4f4 is 0 without stereo, 1 with stereo, 4 with stereo and full-screen stereo, plus 2 for a field-sequential format.
  With FieldsPerFrame at zero the field-array pointer at +0x4f0 is cleared. Otherwise it `calloc`s FieldsPerFrame records of 0x24 bytes ("odsyAttachXvc: Field info alloc failed!") and four word arrays (the first failure prints "Couldn't allocate FieldOffset", the other three "Couldn't allocate FieldSkip", each freeing the arrays made before), reads FieldLineOffset, FieldLineSkip, FieldColor and FieldEye into them with `SDBgetSymbolValueArray`, and for each field copies FieldLineOffset to the record's +0 and FieldLineSkip to +4, FieldColor to +0x1c and FieldEye to +0x20. For +8 to +0x18 of every record it reads the five names FormatInfo_F_0_V_BackPorch, _V_Sync, _V_SyncPulse, _V_FrontPorch and _V_Active (the field-0 names, read again for each record so all records get the same values); on a board whose halfword +0x3e is 1 it instead reads TotalPixelsPerLine and DCDTotalPixelsPerLine and stores for each name DCDTotalPixelsPerLine times the truncated quotient of the value over TotalPixelsPerLine (the sync-pulse value goes to both +0xc and +0x10). The four word arrays are then freed. Next the format name without its last four characters (the file suffix) is copied to +0x3c0, a block of per-format words from +0x558 to +0x5a0 and +0x4fc to +0x504 is cleared or set to 1 or -1 (+0x554 and +0x500 are 1, +0x598 is -1), and `odsyLoadTimingTable..CC` is called with (screen, database, name area, 0, -1) ("odsyAttachXvc: odsyLoadTimingTable Failed"), then `odsyInitGammaMap..ED` ("cannot initialize the gamma map.").
  For a two-head board it halves TotalLinesPerFrame (+0x4c8) and the stored screen width float (+0x3bc), re-reads DCDHorizontalBackPorch, DCDHorizontalFrontPorch, DCDHorizontalSync and DCDTotalPixelsPerLine over +0x4dc, +0x4e4, +0x4e0 and +0x4cc, copies the first head entry to the second (`bcopy`, 0x1f8 bytes at +0x5a4), sets the second head's valid word (+0x78c) to 1 and its float at +0x5a8 to half the screen width. The last head entry takes the compositor words: the board word +0x1cc goes to entry offset +0x1e8, and when +0x1d0 is nonzero the entry's words are cleared (+0x1e0 and +0x154 set to 1) and +0x1d0 replaces the +0x1e8 word. It sets `screenIsSaved` to 1, calls `XSGIvcScreenInit` with the screen and the record and returns 1. The database is closed on every path. On a failure after the allocation it frees the field-record array of each head and returns 0 without freeing the record itself (the listing at 0x1606518 to 0x1606584 shows no free of it).
- `odsyPostInit` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15675e0, 1848 bytes): **established** (the whole listing, 0x15675e0 to 0x1567d14, was read next to the decompilation).
  Sets the screen's physical size in millimetres once the video format is known (argument: the screen; no result). It returns at once, touching nothing, when the monitor-information table pointer `moninfoData` is null (the compare at 0x156763c). Otherwise it first looks, with `strcasecmp`, for a record whose name equals the name at the start of the video-control record, walking `numMonitors` records of 0x4c bytes (loop 0x156767c to 0x15676a4).
  With a match it takes the record's pair list (count at +0x44, pointer at +0x48, 12 bytes a pair: kind word, value word). Pass one copies the value of every kind-6 pair into a 50-word local array; a 51st prints "odsyPostInit: stepping over array boundary!" through `ErrorF` and stops collecting (0x15677c8 to 0x1567830). Pass two, run only when at least one kind-6 value was found, takes each kind-7 pair's value as a candidate width and each kind-6 value as a candidate height (millimetres, as the factor-of-ten paths below show), forms the float ratio candidate-width over candidate-height, subtracts the screen's pixel aspect ratio (`screen +8` over `+0xa`, both signed halfwords, converted to float) and keeps the pair with the smallest absolute difference; the best starts at the float constant 100.0 and an equal difference does not displace an earlier pair. The screen's own halfwords at +0xc and +0xe are the starting width and height, so they stay when no pair is scanned. The winner is stored as width (+0xc, the kind-7 value) and height (+0xe, the kind-6 value) at 0x1567b58, and when the video-control record's word at +4 is 2 the width is doubled (0x1567b64 to 0x1567ba8). This path ends there; the aspect cut below is not applied to it.
  With no matching record it reads the board-private record's pointer at +0x5c. When that record's halfword at +0x3a is not 3 it returns without a change if the halfword at +0x44 is zero, and otherwise sets the width to the byte at +0x5b times 10 and the height to the byte at +0x5c times 10. When the halfword is 3 the record holds one monitor identity block per head, with a valid halfword at +0xc6 (first head) or +0x148 (second head), a width byte at +0xdd or +0x15f and a height byte at +0xde or +0x160. Neither valid returns unchanged. Both valid: the height is the smaller of the two height bytes times 10, and the width is the smaller width byte times 10, or the sum of the two width bytes times 10 when the halfword at +0x3e of the same record is nonzero. Exactly one valid: the height is that head's height byte times 10 and the width is its width byte times 10, or times 20 when the +0x3e halfword is nonzero. All of these paths then reach the final step, which shrinks the width to the height times the pixel aspect ratio (float, truncated) when the width is larger than that (0x1567704 to 0x156775c). That the identity bytes and the table values are physical sizes in centimetres and millimetres is **inferred** from the factor of 10 and the name of the screen fields; the +0x3e halfword being a "heads side by side" flag is **inferred** from its only effect, widening the width.

## Colour-map update routines

- `odsyStoreColors` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16e5800, 1096 bytes): **established.**
  Sends changed colour-map cells of one X colormap to the board (arguments: colormap, number of entries, array of 12-byte X colour items). The visual behind the colormap (first word) has its class in the halfword at +4; a TrueColor visual (class 4) returns at once, as does a colormap with no board slot (its private index word is negative). The colormap's base cell comes from the screen's table at +0x74 of the structure behind the screen's +0x74 (28 bytes an entry, the word at +0x18), indexed by that private word. For a DirectColor visual (class 5) each item names its cells through the visual's three masks and shifts (words +0xc, +0x10 and +0x14 with shifts at +0x18, +0x1c and +0x20): for every component flagged in the item's byte at +10 (bit 0 red, bit 1 green, bit 2 blue) the cell is the base plus the masked and shifted pixel, and the new word keeps the other two 10-bit components from the shadow table (the pointer at +0x54 of the board-private record) and takes the flagged component from the item's 16-bit value masked to 0xffc0 and moved into place. For every other class the cell is the base plus the item's pixel and the word is red in bits 29:20 (the 16-bit value masked to 0xffc0, shifted left 14), green in 19:10 (masked, shifted left 4) and blue in 9:0 (the value shifted right 6); the loop handles two items a pass. The entries are written after a 0x10-byte header in a stack block sized from the item count (a zero from the reservation prints "couldn't allocate memory") and `odsyLoadCmap` receives the block with the count in its first halfword.
- `odsyInstallColormap` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x168c4f0, 916 bytes): **established.**
  Loads a whole colormap into the board when it becomes the installed one (argument: the colormap; no result). A TrueColor visual (class 4) returns at once. The base cell is found as in `odsyStoreColors`. For a DirectColor visual every one of the visual's cells (count at visual +8) gets an entry whose word packs the red, green and blue values of the three per-channel entry arrays (the colormap's words at +0x38, +0x3c and +0x40, 0x14 bytes an entry) as red 29:20, green 19:10, blue 9:0, each masked to 0xffc0 and shifted. For the other classes only cells that are in use are sent: a static visual (class bit 0 clear) sends every cell, a dynamic one only cells whose reference-count halfword at entry +0xc is nonzero and, when the colormap has an allocation bit mask, whose bit in that mask is set; a shared cell's three values are read through the pointers in the entry. The number of entries that went into the block is stored in its first halfword and `odsyLoadCmap` is called once, nothing being sent when no cell qualified.
- `odsyChangeDIDCmap` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1632890, 948 bytes): **established** (the whole listing, 0x1632890 to 0x1632c40, was read next to the decompilation).
  Gives a window the display identifier that matches a new colormap and loads that identifier's display mode (arguments: window, colormap; no result). The window's resource-manager private is the slot `rrmWindowPrivateIndex` of the window's private array (window +0x84); the screen's resource-manager record is the slot `rrmScreenPrivateIndex` of the screen's (+0x1b4). It returns without a change when the private's identifier word at +0x14 is negative, the colormap is null or the colormap's slot number (the word behind the colormap's +0x44) is negative (0x16328f0 to 0x1632908).
  The colormap's cell page is the word at +0x18 of that slot's 28-byte entry in the table behind the screen record's +0x74 (its own +0x74), shifted right 8 (arithmetic). The window's display-mode word at +0x24 of the private holds the page in a bit field that depends on the identifier: below 0x20 the field is bits 8:4 (mask 0x1f0, page shifted left 4), from 0x20 up it is bits 7:3 (mask 0xf8, page shifted left 3). The per-identifier entry is 0x24 bytes at `rrm record + id*0x24`, with a doubly linked window list (head +0x98, tail +0x9c), a flag halfword at +0xa8 and a use count at +0xac. When the entry's flag bit 0 is clear, or the page already in the +0x24 word equals the new one, the routine skips the re-assignment: the +0x24 word and the identifier stay as they are and the mode word sent is the stored word with the field replaced by the new page.
  Otherwise, if the private's flag bit 8 (first halfword) says the window is on its identifier's list, it unlinks it (prev at +0x2c, next at +0x30; with no prev the entry head takes next, with no next the entry tail takes prev, both words cleared), lowers the use count, clears the list flag and recomputes the entry flags as the old bits 0, 4 and 5 (mask 0x31) plus bits 1 and 2 when bit 0 was set, then bit 4 cleared when the list is empty or bits 1 and 2 set when head and tail differ (only bit 1 when they are equal). It then sets the identifier word to -1, stores the new page into the +0x24 word and calls `FindFreeID` with the window; when that returns 0 it calls `StealID` (0x1632a3c to 0x1632c34).
  In every path it then calls the screen record's procedure at +0x20 with (window, 8, 1), builds a mode word from the +0x24 word that has bit 30 set when the private's flag word at +0x20 has bits 1 and 2 both set and cleared when only bit 1 is set (unchanged when bit 1 is clear), and sends `ioctl(descriptor, 0x3f9, block)` (`RRM_LoadDisplayMode`) with the descriptor at +0x64 of the structure behind screen +0x74 and a five-word block: the private's word at +0xc, the window's id (window +4), the identifier now in the private at +0x14, the mode word and the flag word. A negative result prints "odsyChangeDIDCmap: Can't load display mode" and then "rnid=0x%x, clipid=0x%x, errno='%s'" with the first two block words and `strerror(errno)`. Last it stores the identifier, the flag word and the mode word in the board-private record's table behind +0x58, 12 bytes an identifier (0x1632b10 to 0x1632b48). That bit 30 selects the overlay or underlay sense of the mode word is **inferred** from its dependence on the private's two flag bits only.

## Cursor record routines

- `odsyRealizeCursor` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1582940, 1212 bytes): **established.**
  Builds the module's per-screen cursor record for an X cursor (arguments: screen, cursor; result 1, or 0 after a failed allocation). It allocates a 16-byte record with `Xalloc` and stores it in the cursor's word at index screen number + 5 ("odsyRealizeCursor: memory allocation failed" on failure), allocates the 0x300-byte glyph and alpha buffer (the pointer goes to +8; a failure frees the record, prints "nfbRealizeCursor: memory allocation failed" and returns 0), zeroes the buffer, and sets the record's word at +0 and +4 to 0 and the halfword at +0xc to 1. Width and height come from the cursor bits' halfwords at +8 and +10, each limited to 32. For every row it fills 16 bytes of glyph (four 32-bit words at the row's offset in the first 512 bytes) and 8 bytes of alpha (two words at +0x200 in the buffer): the row's mask word, cut by a left-aligned width mask, is expanded to two bits a pixel with value 3 where the mask bit is set (the 16-iteration loop that shifts 0x8000 right), and the source word ANDed with that masked word goes through the 16-word nibble table at rodata offset 0xb70 (the table in `odsyDisplayCursor`) for the glyph words, which hold 1 where a source-and-mask bit is set. This is the same fill as the table path of `odsyDisplayCursor`; because the record's second-colour pointer at +4 stays 0, the other path of that routine never applies to a cursor made here.
- `nfbUnrealizeCursor` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x168ae50, 208 bytes): **established.**
  Releases a cursor's record for a screen (arguments: screen, cursor; result 1). The record is the cursor's word at index screen number + 5; if there is none the routine returns 1 at once (0x168aef4). The glyph buffer at +8 is freed with `Xfree`; when that pointer is null the message "WSGO! Unrealize nocache cursor with no data!" is printed instead. The object at +4 is freed when present, the record is freed, the slot in the cursor is cleared and 1 is returned.
- `nfbCursorLimits` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16b8160, 84 bytes): **established.**
  Clips a cursor's box to the screen (arguments: screen, unused, input box of four 16-bit values x1, y1, x2, y2, output box). x1 and y1 are limited below by 0, x2 is limited above by the screen's width halfword at +8 and y2 by its height halfword at +10; the output box holds the four results and nothing else is touched.
- `nfbRecolorCursor` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x162d0f0, 56 bytes): **established.**
  Forwards a cursor recolour request (arguments: screen, a second argument it does not read, cursor). When the cursor is null it returns without doing anything (the branch at 0x162d104); otherwise it calls the procedure stored at +0x11c of the screen with its arguments unchanged (the `jalr` at 0x162d110 after `lw t9,0x11c(a0)`).

## Screen initialisation

- `odsyInit` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x156f890, 5208 bytes): **established** (the whole listing, 0x156f890 to 0x1570ce4, was read next to the decompilation; the earlier reading was corrected where noted).
  The module's screen initialiser (arguments include the screen at the second position; result 1, or 0 after any failure, each of which frees what was allocated). On the first screen of a server generation it allocates the module's screen and graphics-context private indexes (`odsyScreenPrivateIndex`, `odsyGCPrivateIndex`), then an 8-byte graphics-context private and a 0x140-byte board-private record that it zeroes, sets the word at +0x13c to 1 (the cmap-send enable `odsyLoadCmap` tests) clears the words at +0x74, +0x78 and +0x88 and points +0x5c at the board-information record from `ddxGetBoardInfo` (called with the byte at +0xc of the screen's board structure) plus 0x28. If the generation check fails because an index allocation returns a negative number, or the 0x140-byte allocation fails, the routine returns 0 at once. It calls `odsyDefaultTTExists` with the six output slots in its stack frame for the starting format ("No default timing table and framebuffer config!!!!" when none is found), stores the returned sizes, depth code and index (record +0x10, +0x14, +0x18, +0x1c, and the width in 16-pixel units at +0xc), and calls `odsyKernInit..O` to start the kernel side (a result other than 1 returns 0). From the board's display-record bytes at +0xc, +0xe, +0xf and +8 and +0xa it sets the default visual class, depth, id and overlay choice (+0x2c to +0x38), taking the server defaults `ddxDfltClass`, `ddxDfltDepth` and `ddxDfltVisualID` when a byte is 0xff or 0, and 1 for the overlay when `ddxOverlayFlag` is set. It allocates 23 hardware colour-map groups with `nfbInitHWCmapArrays`; the depth index indexes a rodata word table at 0x680 that gives the visual-mask bit to test (entries 0, 0, 1 and 2 for indexes 0 to 3, so indexes 0 and 1 print "odsyInit: unsupported FBdepth index: %d" and fail), and two loops count the entries of the module's capability table (`odsyCapabilityInfo`) that suit the board (the board record's word at +0x50 and +0x34 and the mask bits tested against 0x30 and 0x100) to size the arrays it allocates next (0x24, 0x14 and 0x44 bytes an entry, two 0x2640-byte colour tables and four word tables for the shadow and valid-region data: "failed to alloc shadow colormap", "failed to alloc validRegions"). It then runs `nfbGenericScreenInit` ("nfbGenericScreenInit failed"), allocates the screen-private index of the zero-width line code once per generation, runs `nfbHWColormapScreenInit` with the colour-map group arrays, allocates a 0x8000-byte shadow colour table stored at +0x54 of the board record ("failed to alloc shadow colormap", which also frees the group arrays), reads the display register information with `ReadDisplayRegisterInfo`, and runs `odsyAttachXvc` with the starting format name, `odsyBlackCmap`, `rrmInit` (the board-private word +0x68 first receives the descriptor from `odsyKernInit..O`) and `odsyInitDefaultHW..N`. Each failure from `nfbHWColormapScreenInit` on frees the board record and the group arrays and returns 0. It then makes the client id for the default colormap with `FakeClientID(0)` and creates the colormap with `rrmCreateDefColormap`, clears the module's per-region cache table, and, the first time only, reads the machine's inventory with `scaninvent` through `odsyScanAudioInventory..K` (a negative result prints "scaninvent can't read inventory table" and returns 0); when the inventory word is 0 it installs `odsyBell` and `odsyKeyClick` in the screen's procedures at +0xc and +0x10, otherwise it sets the word to 1. It then runs `glxScreenInit` with `odsyglxScreenPriv`, allocates the 0x18-byte block stored at +0x40 of the board record and initialises two regions in it with the screen's region procedure at +0x154, and finally reads these environment variables, each applied with `atoi` when set and nonempty and stored in a module word: `ODSY_DMA_THRESHOLD` (0x17345e0) and `ODSY_PIO_THRESHOLD` (0x17345e4), the thresholds of `odsyDrawImageSingleBuffer..J`, `ODSY_REPLICATE_TILE_THRESHOLD` (0x17345d8), `ODSY_REPLICATE_TILE_FOR_CP_THRESHOLD` (0x17345dc) and `ODSY_COPY_WINDOW_WAR` (0x1738220). `ODSY_RECT_SPAN_WIDTH_THRESHOLD` is looked up and converted with `atoi`, but no store follows the call at 0x1570910, so its value is discarded and setting it has no effect. What the four stored thresholds other than the first two control is not read in this routine. The failure messages seen in the listing are "odsyInit: No default timing table and framebuffer config!!!!", "unsupported FBdepth index", "nfbGenericScreenInit failed", "failed to alloc shadow colormap", "failed to alloc validRegions" and "scaninvent can't read inventory table".

## Window display-identifier routines

The resource-manager client keeps one record per screen (the screen-private slot `rrmScreenPrivateIndex`) and one record per window (the window-private slot `rrmWindowPrivateIndex`). The routines below all use the same fields, read from their listings. Screen record: the number of display identifiers at +0, a mask for the window's first mode word at +0x10 and for its second at +0x14, the flag word at +0xc (bit 0 is tested by `FindFreeID`), the word at +0x30 (nonzero allows reserved entries to be reclaimed), procedures at +0x20 (called with a window, 8 and 1 to evict it) and +0x48 (called with a window and its previous identifier after an assignment), and an array of 0x24-byte identifier entries from +0x98: the head and tail of the entry's window list (+0x98, +0x9c), two mode words (+0xa0, +0xa4), a flag halfword (+0xa8), the entry's window class (halfword +0xaa) and the number of windows on it (+0xac). Window record: a flag halfword at +0 whose bit 3 means "on an identifier list", the window class (halfword +2), the identifier (word +0x14, -1 for none), two mode words (+0x20, +0x24) and the list links (+0x2c previous, +0x30 next). The entry flag bits are used as follows: bit 0 marks a reserved entry (`FindFreeID` tests it, and unlinking turns a reserved entry's bits 1 and 2 on); bit 1 is set by an assignment and means in use; bit 2 means shared (set by a second assignment, and by an unlink while head and tail differ); bit 4 is cleared by an unlink that empties the list; bit 5 is kept through an unlink and blocks the reclaim in `FindFreeID`. These meanings are **inferred** from the code that sets and tests the bits. Every routine below unlinks a window with the same sequence: the previous window's next link (or the entry head) takes the window's next, the next window's previous link (or the entry tail) takes the window's previous, both links are cleared, the entry's count is lowered, the entry flags become the old bits 0, 4 and 5 plus bits 1 and 2 when bit 0 was set, then bit 4 cleared when the list is empty or bit 1 (head equals tail) or bits 1 and 2 (they differ) set, and the window's list flag is cleared.

- `AssignID..FG` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15e96a0, 308 bytes): **established** (whole listing 0x15e96a0 to 0x15e97d0).
  Puts a window on a display identifier's list (arguments: window, identifier; no result of its own, it ends by calling a screen procedure). It stores the identifier in the window record's +0x14 and in the word at +4 of the window's frame-buffer private (slot `nfbWindowPrivateIndex`), remembering the old value of that word, clears the window's next link, sets its previous link to the entry's tail and appends it (the entry head when the list was empty, otherwise the old tail's next link), sets the window's list flag, counts the window in the entry (+0xac) and ORs 2 into the entry flags. When the count was 0 before (the first window) and the entry's bit 0 is clear it calls `SetDIDMode` for the window first; when the count becomes 2 or more it ORs 6 into the flags. It finally jumps to the screen record's procedure at +0x48 with the window and the old frame-buffer word (a tail call at 0x15e9784 and 0x15e97a8).
- `NewID..GG` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x162eb60, 296 bytes): **established** (whole listing 0x162eb60 to 0x162ec84).
  Moves a window to a different display identifier (arguments: window, new identifier; the second argument is passed through untouched to the next routine). When the window already has an identifier (word +0x14 not negative) and is on a list, it unlinks the window from its present entry with the sequence above; it then sets the window's identifier word to -1 and tail-calls `AssignID..FG` with the window and the new identifier (0x162ec58). A window with no identifier goes straight to `AssignID..FG` (0x162ec60).
- `cmapDID` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1636ba0, 336 bytes): **established** (whole listing 0x1636ba0 to 0x1636cec).
  Returns the display identifier that goes with a window's colormap (argument: window; result the identifier). The window's record at +0x78 (made by a helper called with the window when it is null, 0x1636bd0) gives a colormap id at +8; a window whose class byte (+1) is 2 uses id 0. `LookupIDByType(id, 6)` finds the colormap; when it exists and its first slot word (behind +0x44) is not negative, the result is word +0x10 of that slot's 28-byte entry in the table behind the screen record's +0x74. Otherwise the record's first word (a visual number) minus the base in the screen structure's +0x50 indexes the table at +0x58 (0x14 bytes an entry), and the result is that entry's first word. That +0x78 names a visual record, and the +0x58 table is per visual, is **inferred** from the arithmetic.
- `FindBestID` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x157e810, 656 bytes): **established** (whole listing 0x157e810 to 0x157ea9c).
  Chooses the display identifier for a window whose class or colormap may have changed (argument: window; no result). It scans all identifiers: an entry matches when its class halfword equals the window's class and, under the masks at +0x10 and +0x14 of the screen record, the window's two mode words equal the entry's two. The last matching entry is the candidate, except that a matching entry with flag bit 2 set is taken at once: if the window already has that identifier the routine returns, otherwise the window is unlinked, its identifier word set to -1 and `AssignID..FG` is called with that entry (0x157e980 to 0x157ea50). With a candidate that has no bit 2, every window on the candidate's list is evicted by calling the screen procedure at +0x20 with (window, 0, 1) (the loop at 0x157e920, which follows each window's next link), then `NewID..GG` moves this window to the candidate. With no match it takes the identifier from `cmapDID` and calls `NewID..GG` unless the window already has it (0x157ea5c to 0x157ea7c).
- `FindFreeID` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x171e8c0, 920 bytes): **established** (whole listing 0x171e8c0 to 0x171ec54).
  Gives a window a display identifier no other window uses (argument: window; result 1 when it has one, 0 when none could be found). A class-1 window on a screen whose flag word has bit 0 set takes the identifier equal to the identifier count (the entry past the table) and returns 1 (0x171ebf8). A window that already has an identifier whose entry has flag bit 2 clear keeps it and returns 1. Otherwise the first entry of the window's class that has flag bit 0 clear and no windows on it takes the window (after an unlink from the old entry and the identifier word set to -1, `AssignID..FG` is called, result 1). If none exists and the screen record's word +0x30 is nonzero, a second scan finds an entry of the class with bit 0 set (reserved), bits 5 and 1 clear and no windows, zeroes that entry's flag halfword and assigns the window to it (result 1); otherwise the result is 0.
- `SetReservedID` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1719690, 1612 bytes): **established** (whole listing 0x1719690 to 0x1719cd8).
  Turns a display identifier into a reserved one and re-homes the windows that were on it (arguments: screen, identifier; no result). It first takes every window off the entry's list: the loop at 0x171976c saves the list head in a stack array, calls the screen record's procedure at +0x20 with (window, 0, 0) when the window record's flag byte at +6 has bit 3 set (halfword bit 11), unlinks it from its present entry with the common sequence and sets its identifier word to -1. Then the entry's two mode words (+0xa0, +0xa4) are replaced by the words at +0xb0 and +0xb4 of the same entry, the screen record's procedure at +0x2c is called with (screen, identifier) (its job is **unknown**: it is called through a table slot the module does not name here), and the entry's flags get bit 1 set. Each saved window is then given a new identifier by what is `FindFreeID`'s logic written inline: a class-1 window on a screen whose flag word has bit 0 set takes the identifier equal to the identifier count; a window that has no identifier, or whose entry has flag bit 2, takes the first entry of its class with bit 0 clear and no windows (`AssignID..FG`), or else a reserved unused entry that it clears (only when the record's word +0x30 is nonzero), or else `StealID` (0x17198e4 and 0x1719af0). If the entry's old flags had bit 4 set (0x10) that bit is ORed into the flags of the window's new entry and the procedure at +0x20 is called with (window, 8, 1) (0x17198ec to 0x1719930).
- `FindFreeCID` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15b8810, 2628 bytes): **established** (whole listing 0x15b8810 to 0x15b9244 and its tail blocks; the three assignment blocks differ only in which entry they use).
  Gives a window a clip identifier, the analogue of `FindFreeID` for the screen record's second table (argument: window; no result). The clip-identifier table is at +0x94 of the screen record (0x1c-byte entries: list head, list tail, flag halfword at +8, class halfword at +0xa, window count at +0xc), the count is at +4 and a round-robin cursor at +0x88; the window record keeps its clip identifier at +0x18, its list links at +0x3c (previous) and +0x40 (next) and its on-list flag as bit 6 (0x40) of its first halfword. A screen procedure at +0x5c is called with the window; when it returns 0 the window takes the identifier equal to the count (word at +4) and the routine returns (0x15b886c, 0x15b904c). Otherwise a window that already has an identifier whose entry has flag bit 2 clear keeps it. Else it scans the entries other than the window's own for one of the window's class (or, for a class-1 window, any entry when the screen flag word has bit 1 set) with flag bit 0 clear: the first one with no windows is taken at once (the old entry is left by the unlink, which keeps bit 0, adds 4 for a reserved entry, sets 2 when the list is not empty and 4 when its head and tail differ), the window is appended to the entry's list, its on-list flag is set, the count is raised and 2 ORed into the flags (6 when the count reaches 2). An entry in use is remembered as a victim when the byte at +4 of its head window's record differs from the byte at +6. Without a free entry, the last victim (or, with no victim, the entry that the round-robin cursor reaches after it is incremented and wrapped to 1 when it passes the count) is cleared: its head window gets the screen procedure at +0x20 with (window, 2, 0), is unlinked and put on entry 0's list with clip identifier 0, and the requesting window is unlinked from its own entry and appended to the victim's (0x15b8958 to 0x15b9244). That the head-window bytes at +4 and +6 are a pending-versus-current state is **inferred**.

## Empty and constant-result routines of the shared X code

Each of these is a whole routine of two instructions, `jr ra` and the delay slot, read in full; none touches memory except where it says so.

- `ZBuffer..LF` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x158f300, 8 bytes): **established.**
  A void routine that does nothing: `jr ra` at 0x158f300 and a no-op at 0x158f304, so its arguments are never read and no result register is set.
- `nfb5RopLoop..FH` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16562e0, 8 bytes): **established.**
  Another empty body, the return at 0x16562e0 with a no-op after it; this is the placeholder behind the five-operand raster-op loop slot of the frame-buffer layer, and it leaves every register as it found it.
- `nfb5MaskRopLoop..FI` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1718210, 8 bytes): **established.**
  The masked sibling of the previous routine is just as empty: `jr ra` at 0x1718210, no-op at 0x1718214, nothing read or written.
- `miChangeGC` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15fd090, 8 bytes): **established.**
  The machine-independent graphics-context change hook is a bare return at 0x15fd090 (no-op delay slot): a change to the context's fields is not acted on by this routine.
- `miCopyGC` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16c0410, 8 bytes): **established.**
  The context-copy hook likewise only returns (0x16c0410, no-op at 0x16c0414), so copying one context to another has no side effect here.
- `nfbChangeGC..LJ` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1620dc0, 8 bytes): **established.**
  The frame-buffer layer's context-change entry is an immediate return at 0x1620dc0 and does nothing with the context it is given.
- `nfbCopyGC..BK` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1669fb0, 8 bytes): **established.**
  The frame-buffer layer's context-copy entry is also an immediate return (0x1669fb0, no-op delay slot).
- `mfbUnrealizeFont` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15bfe30, 8 bytes): **established.**
  Reports success without touching the font: `jr ra` at 0x15bfe30 with `li v0,1` in the delay slot at 0x15bfe34.
- `mfbRealizeFont` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x162ca40, 8 bytes): **established.**
  Also answers 1 and nothing else (`li v0,1` at 0x162ca44 behind the return at 0x162ca40); the monochrome layer keeps no realised-font data here.
- `nfbPositionWindow` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16afbc0, 8 bytes): **established.**
  Window repositioning always succeeds without work: the result is the constant 1 loaded at 0x16afbc4, and neither the window nor the screen is read.
- `nfbUnrealizeWindow` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16fd920, 8 bytes): **established.**
  The unmap hook returns 1 (0x16fd924) and does nothing else.
- `nfbNoopDDACreateScreenResources` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16f5ea0, 8 bytes): **established.**
  The device-dependent screen-resource creation step of the frame-buffer layer is a stub that returns 1 (`li v0,1` at 0x16f5ea4), so no resources are allocated through this slot.
- `nfbNoopDDATranslateBackingStore` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x160d120, 8 bytes): **established.**
  Returns 0 (the delay slot at 0x160d124 is `or v0,zero,zero`) and reads nothing: no backing-store translation is offered.
- `nfbNoopDDAClearBackingStore` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x171dbd0, 8 bytes): **established.**
  Returns 0 (0x171dbd4) with no other action, the same shape as the translate stub above.
- `miRegionExtents` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15e5ab0, 8 bytes): **established.**
  Hands back its first argument unchanged (`or v0,a0,zero` at 0x15e5ab4); that is the address of the bounding box because the box is the first member of a region record.
- `miGetLayerWindow` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16e39f0, 8 bytes): **established.**
  Returns the word at +0x24 of the window record (`lw v0,0x24(a0)` at 0x16e39f4), which is the window's parent pointer in the X server's `WindowRec`, so the layer window of a window is its parent here.

## Forwarding routines (audio library, format-file finders, symbol-database calls)

The audio-library and symbol-database routines are statically linked library code inside the module (`libaudio` and a symbol-table package); the entries give what the code at the address does. Where a routine forwards, its arguments reach the target unchanged unless the entry says otherwise; the target is a static routine without a dynamic symbol, named by address.

- `ALnewconfig` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1550410, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Old-style audio-library name for creating a configuration object; the body builds `gp`, then `bgezall` at 0x155042c transfers to `alNewConfig` at 0x1551960 with the argument registers untouched and returns its result.
- `ALfreeconfig` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1550450, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Forwards to `alFreeConfig` at 0x1551a10 through the same `bgezall` jump (0x155046c); no argument is changed.
- `ALsetconfig` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1550490, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Jumps (0x15504ac) to `alSetConfig` at 0x1552450 with its two arguments as received.
- `ALgetconfig` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15504d0, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Jumps (0x15504ec) to `alGetConfig` at 0x1552740 and returns what it returns.
- `ALsetwidth` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1550510, 52 bytes): **established.**
  A real call rather than a jump: `jalr` at 0x155052c to `alSetWidth` (0x1561cb0) from a frame that saves `gp` and `ra`, then returns the callee's result.
- `ALgetwidth` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1550550, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Calls `alGetWidth` at 0x15624c0 (`jalr` at 0x155056c) and passes the result back.
- `ALsetfloatmax` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1550690, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Calls `alSetFloatMax` at 0x1562320 (`jalr` at 0x15506ac) and returns its result; the argument is a floating-point maximum, passed through in the argument registers.
- `ALopenport` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1550750, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Jumps (0x155076c) to `alOpenPort` at 0x1552e80 with the port name, direction and configuration it was given.
- `ALgetfd` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1550790, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Jumps (0x15507ac) to `alGetFD` at 0x1552290, which yields the port's file descriptor.
- `ALseterrorhandler` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15516a0, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Jumps (0x15516bc) to `alSetErrorHandler` at 0x15518e0, the routine below that installs the handler.
- `alSetErrorHandler` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15518e0, 64 bytes): **established.**
  Replaces the library's error-handler pointer, a global word (0x1742360). It loads the old pointer and compares it with the library's built-in default at 0x1551860; if the old value is that default, the value returned is 1 instead of the pointer. The new value is stored unless the argument is 1, in which case the default is put back. So 1 stands for "default handler" in both directions.
- `alGetErrorString` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1551920, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Maps an error number to its text: for a number from 0 to 26 (the signed compares at 0x1551920 and 0x1551934) it returns the pointer stored at that index of the 27-entry table at 0x1732558; any other number returns 0.
- `ALgetfilled` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1550250, 76 bytes): **established.**
  Number of samples waiting in a port's ring buffer. It reads two pointers from the port record (+0x10 and +0x14), takes the difference of the words they point to, adds the ring size at +8 when that is negative, then scales by shifting right by the word at +0x80 and left by the word at +0x7c (bytes to sample units).
- `ALgetfillable` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15501f0, 84 bytes): **established.**
  The free space in the same ring: it takes the word behind the +0x10 pointer minus the word behind the +0x14 pointer (the opposite order to `ALgetfilled`), subtracts a reserve held at +0x58, wrapped by the ring size at +8 when negative and scaled by the same two shift words at +0x80 and +0x7c.
- `alGetParams` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1562af0, 60 bytes): **established.**
  Calls the shared parameter reader `__alParams` at 0x1562560 with three constants added after the caller's arguments: 1 in the fourth register and -1 and 1 in the fifth and sixth; it is the "get" flavour of that reader.
- `_AL2WriteNToN_ADAT` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15559c0, 60 bytes): **established.**
  Copies sample frames between buffers unchanged: `bcopy` (0x15559e4) with the byte count equal to the frame count shifted left by the converter record's word at +0x80 plus 2 (four bytes a channel scaled by the record's shift).
- `_AL2WriteNToN_AES` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1555970, 72 bytes): **established.**
  Same straight copy for the 3-byte-per-sample AES format: the byte count is frames times three (`sll`+`addu` forms 3n at 0x155598c to 0x155599c), shifted by the record's word at +0x80 plus 2, then `bcopy`.
- `_AL2WriteNToN_AESv` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15599e0, 60 bytes): **established.**
  Triples the fourth argument (`sll`/`addu` at 0x15599f8 to 0x1559a04) and jumps to `_AL2WriteNToN_24v` at 0x1559a20: the voiced AES writer is the 24-bit one with the channel count scaled to bytes.
- `_AL2ReadNFromN_ADAT` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1556f00, 72 bytes): **established.**
  The reverse copy: swaps the first two arguments (0x1556f18 to 0x1556f24) so source and destination trade places, then `bcopy`s frames shifted left by the record's +0x80 plus 2.
- `_AL2ReadNFromN_AES` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1556ea0, 84 bytes): **established.**
  The 3-byte-sample reader, the mirror of `_AL2WriteNToN_AES`: the source and destination arguments are swapped into the `bcopy` call (0x1556eb4, 0x1556ec0, 0x1556ecc) and the byte count is three times the frame count, shifted left by the converter record's word at +0x80 plus 2.
- `_AL2ReadNFromN_AESv` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x155d2c0, 60 bytes): **established.**
  Multiplies the fourth argument by three (0x155d2d8 to 0x155d2e4) and jumps to `_AL2ReadNFromN_24v` at 0x155d300; the voiced AES reader is the 24-bit reader with a byte-sized stride.
- `_AL2Read4ChanFromStereo_16` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1558cc0, 84 bytes): **established.**
  Expands a stereo stream of 32-bit words into four-channel 16-bit frames. For each of the count (third argument) input pairs it stores the left word shifted right by 8 at +0 of the output frame, the right word shifted right by 8 at +2, and zero at +4 and +6 (the loop at 0x1558ce0 to 0x1558d08); the output advances by 8 bytes a frame.
- `_AL2Read4ChanFromStereo_8` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1558d20, 84 bytes): **established.**
  The 8-bit form of the same expansion: input words shifted right by 16 become bytes 0 and 1 of a 4-byte output frame, bytes 2 and 3 are zero (loop 0x1558d40 to 0x1558d68).
- `_AL2recalcscalefactors` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1552e30, 80 bytes): **established.**
  Recomputes six cached conversion factors from the sample rate stored as a double at +0x48 of the converter record. With r the rate: doubles at +0x98 and +0xa0 get r times 2^-23 and r times 2^-24 (the constants 1.1920929e-07 and 5.9604645e-08 at 0x173f740 and 0x173f748), the double at +0xa8 gets 2^23 divided by r (8388608.0), and floats at +0x88, +0x8c and +0x90 hold the narrowed copies of the same three.
- `nfbInfPolyText8..CL` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16cd920, 76 bytes): **established.**
  Dispatches the 8-bit text request to the context's own operation table. The context (second argument) holds its device-private array at +0x4c; the entry selected by `nfbGCPrivateIndex` is a record whose pointer at +0x20 leads to a table, and the routine calls that table's entry at +0x34 with its arguments unchanged (0x16cd938 to 0x16cd954).
- `nfbInfPolyText16..DL` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15aefa0, 76 bytes): **established.**
  Same indirection for 16-bit text, calling the table entry at +0x38 (the load at 0x15aefd0).
- `nfbInfImageText8..EL` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15ba450, 76 bytes): **established.**
  Fills in background and glyphs of 8-bit text through the table entry at +0x3c (0x15ba480), reached from the context's private record in the same way.
- `nfbInfImageText16..FL` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1717260, 76 bytes): **established.**
  Same dispatch for 16-bit image text, through the entry at +0x40 (0x1717290).
- `nfbInfPushPixels..IL` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16f65c0, 76 bytes): **established.**
  Here the context is the first argument (0x16f65dc reads its private array), and the table entry at +0x4c is called (0x16f65f0).
- `nfbInfCopyArea..IK` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1640300, 84 bytes): **established.**
  The context is the third argument; the call goes to table entry +0xc (0x1640338). The seventh argument sits on the caller's stack and is re-stored at the new frame's first stack slot (0x1640310 to 0x1640318) so the callee sees it.
- `nfbInfCopyPlane..JK` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x160c350, 92 bytes): **established.**
  As the copy-area dispatcher, through table entry +0x10 (0x160c388), with two stack arguments re-stored (0x160c35c to 0x160c374).
- `miBSCheapDestroyGC..NCB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1701c20, 76 bytes): **established.**
  Undoes the backing-store wrapper on a context. It fetches the wrapper's private record through a module-private index (0x1701c38) from the context's device-private array, stores that record's pointer into the context at +0x44 (its operation-table slot, restoring the saved original table) and calls the original destroy entry at +0xc of it.
- `cfb32CopyPlane` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16543d0, 72 bytes): **established.**
  Not a copy at all: it jumps through the function-pointer variable `cfbPuntCopyPlane` (0x1742364, loaded at 0x16543f4), re-storing the two stack arguments at 0x16543d8 and 0x16543e4 for the callee; the 32-bit colour layer hands plane copies to whatever routine the variable names.
- `cfb16CopyPlane` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x165e960, 72 bytes): **established.**
  Identical in effect for the 16-bit layer: calls the routine in `cfbPuntCopyPlane` (0x165e984 to 0x165e990) after re-storing two stack arguments.
- `cfb32DoBitblt` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x158f790, 48 bytes): **established.**
  Chooses between two block-transfer routines by the plane mask (eighth argument): when the mask is all ones (`xor` with -1 at 0x158f7a4 gives zero) it takes the copy-only routine `cfb32DoBitbltCopy` (0x1722230), otherwise `cfb32DoBitbltGeneral` (0x16c1ff0), through `movz` at 0x158f7ac, and jumps there with an extra argument 3 in the third slot.
- `cfbDoBitblt` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16abad0, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Eight-bit-layer version: the test is the low byte of the plane mask against 0xff (0x16abae0 to 0x16abae8); a full mask selects `cfbDoBitbltCopy` (0x157d2f0), anything else `cfbDoBitbltGeneral` (0x166de60).
- `cfb16DoBitblt` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x171b400, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Sixteen-bit version: low halfword of the mask against 0xffff (0x171b410 to 0x171b418), a full mask goes to `cfb16DoBitbltCopy` (0x1719ce0, loaded at 0x171b404), anything else to `cfb16DoBitbltGeneral` (0x16c92c0, loaded at 0x171b40c).
- `nfbSaveAreas` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1688a10, 80 bytes): **established.**
  Backing-store save is a block copy: it rearranges its arguments (0x1688a2c to 0x1688a40) and calls `nfbDoBitBltSP` (0x1708840) with a raster-op of 3 (copy) and a trailing -1 (all planes).
- `nfbSaveScreen` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1669300, 64 bytes): **established.**
  Screen-saver hook: calls the screen's entry at +0xc0 of the screen record held at +0x74 of the first argument with (is-the-mode-zero, screen) (`sltiu a0,a1,1` at 0x1669320), so mode 0 ("on") passes 1 and every other mode passes 0.
- `nfbDestroyHWColormap` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1718220, 68 bytes): **established.**
  Frees the hardware colour-map's data block: `Xfree` of the pointer at +0x44 (0x1718244) and then clears that field (0x1718254).
- `nfbGetBestVisualForPEX` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1586c60, 24 bytes): **established.**
  Answers the 3-D extension's visual query from fixed screen words: it copies the word at +0xcc of the record at +0x74 of its first argument to the second output pointer and the word at +0xd0 to the third (0x1586c60 to 0x1586c74); it makes no choice itself.
- `nfbAbort` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15e5a70, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Terminates the server on an impossible state: it builds a 16-byte frame saving `gp` and `ra` (0x15e5a74 to 0x15e5a7c) and calls C library `abort` through the call at 0x15e5a8c, with no argument and no code after the call that depends on a result.
- `miCloseScreen` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x166f6b0, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Calls the screen's destroy-colour-map entry at +0xf0 of the screen record (second argument) with the word at +0x74 of that record as its argument (0x166f6c4, 0x166f6cc) and returns whatever it returns.
- `miRegionNotEmpty` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1710230, 40 bytes): **established.**
  Reports whether a region contains rectangles: with no data block at +8 it answers 1 (the box alone is a rectangle); with a block it answers 0 when the block's rectangle count at +4 is zero, else 1 (0x1710234 to 0x1710250).
- `miRegionEmpty` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1685cc0, 100 bytes): **established.**
  Turns a region into the empty region. It frees the rectangle block at +8 when there is one with a nonzero size word (0x1685cdc to 0x1685cf4), points +8 at the module's shared empty block (`gp`+0x4d50, 0x1685cfc to 0x1685d04), and collapses the bounding box to a point by copying its first corner (x at +0, y at +2) over its second corner (+4 and +6, 0x1685d0c to 0x1685d20).
- `miRegionReset` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1586a50, 116 bytes): **established.**
  Makes a region a single rectangle: the four halfwords of the box passed as second argument are copied over the region's bounding box (0x1586a64 to 0x1586a88), a rectangle block at +8 with a nonzero size word is passed to `Xfree` (0x1586aa0), and +8 is zeroed (0x1586ac0), which is the one-rectangle form.
- `miRegionDestroy` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x163c1f0, 88 bytes): **established.**
  Frees a region: the rectangle block at +8, when it exists and its size word is nonzero (0x163c20c to 0x163c224), then the region record itself with a second `Xfree` (0x163c230).
- `miRegionUninit` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1665db0, 72 bytes): **established.**
  Frees only the rectangle block of a region when it exists and has a nonzero size word (0x1665dc8 to 0x1665de4); the region record itself is left alone, which is the difference from `miRegionDestroy`.
- `cfbDestroyPixmap` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16cd8c0, 88 bytes): **established.**
  Drops one reference: the count at +0x18 is decremented and stored (0x16cd8cc to 0x16cd8dc); only when it reaches zero is the pixmap passed to `Xfree` (0x16cd8f4 to 0x16cd8fc). The result is always 1.
- `miModifyPixmapHeader` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16e3a00, 104 bytes): **established.**
  Rewrites a pixmap's header in place (result 0 for a null pixmap). Depth goes to the byte at +2 and bits per pixel to +3, the id (+4) and the origin (+8, +0xa) are zeroed, width and height go to +0xc and +0xe, the pitch to +0x1c and the data pointer to +0x20, and the reference count at +0x18 becomes 1. The serial number at +0x14 takes the next value of a module counter (increment, wrap to 1 once it passes 0x1000000, 0x16e3a48 to 0x16e3a64).
- `miZeroDashLine` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16780f0, 72 bytes): **established.**
  Draws thin dashed lines by calling the wide-dash routine `miWideDash` (0x1704240) with the context's halfword at +6 set to 1 for the call and restored to 0 afterwards (0x16780fc and 0x1678128), so the wide-line code runs in its zero-width mode.

## Symbol-database package and format-file finders

The module links a small symbol-database package (`SDB*` and its `xdr_SDB*` encoders). Its symbol table is an array of 0x2c-byte entries held through the database record (array pointer at +0xac, entry count at +0x78): +0 data type, +4 element count, +8 file offset, +0xc a 32-byte name. A static lookup at 0x154f08c copies the name, runs `bsearch` over the array with `compareSTEName` and, on a hit, calls the database's file-access entry (+0x14 of its method table) with the entry's offset; on a miss it sets `errno` to 6 and returns 0.
