- `compareSTEName` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154f774, 56 bytes): **established.**
  The comparison function for that search: `strcmp` of the two names at +0xc of each entry (both pointers advanced by 12 at 0x154f78c and 0x154f798).
- `SDBgetSymbolDatatype` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154f1f4, 88 bytes): **established.**
  Looks the name up through the static search and returns the entry's first word (data type); 0 when the name is not in the table.
- `SDBgetSymbolElementCount` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154f24c, 92 bytes): **established.**
  Returns the entry's element count at +4; for a name that is not in the table it returns the stack word at the frame's first slot (a3 loaded at 0x154f274 and moved to the result at 0x154f298), which is whatever the caller left there.
- `SDBgetSymbolDataAddress` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154f18c, 104 bytes): **established.**
  Looks the name up and, when found, calls the database method at +0x10 of its method table and returns its result (the data address in the backing store); returns 0 when the name is absent (0x154f1b4 to 0x154f1dc).
- `SDBgetSymbolValueScalar` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154f2e0, 56 bytes): **established.**
  Forwards to the static typed-value reader at 0x154f12c with a fourth argument of 0 (0x154f304); that reader searches the name and decodes the value through the `xdr_typedData` routine with the entry's type.
- `SDBgetSymbolValueArray` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154f2a8, 56 bytes): **established.**
  Forwards to the same reader at 0x154f12c unchanged, so an array symbol is read with the caller's own count argument.
- `SDBaddNewScalarSymbol` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154ec24, 64 bytes): **established.**
  Adds a one-value symbol by calling the static add routine at 0x154e92c, shifting the value argument up and passing element count 0 (0x154ec44 to 0x154ec50). That routine allocates an entry, copies the 32-byte name, stores the type and count, asks the database method at +0x10 for a file offset and writes the value with `xdr_typedData`.
- `SDBaddNewArraySymbol` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154ebec, 56 bytes): **established.**
  Calls the same add routine at 0x154e92c with the caller's arguments unchanged (0x154ec0c), the count argument now carrying the array length.
- `SDBsetupMemoryInterface` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154febc, 36 bytes): **established.**
  Stores its four arguments into a four-word global table (offsets 0, 4, 8 and 0xc, 0x154fec8 to 0x154fedc), the package's installable memory and file hooks; nothing is called.
- `SDBinitializeSymbolTableEntries` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154f9b8, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Clears the first three words and the name's first byte of each of the count entries (second argument) of a 0x2c-byte table, walking 0x2c bytes a step (0x154f9c4 to 0x154f9e0); an empty table returns at once.
- `SDBsetHeader` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154ef5c, 120 bytes): **established.**
  Fills the database header held at +8 of the record: the two halfwords of the version argument go to +8 and +0xa (zeroed when the argument is null), and the description string (an empty default at `gp`+0x4e7c when null) is copied with `strncpy` of 100 bytes to +0xc (0x154ef94 to 0x154efbc).
- `xdr_SDBelementIndex` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154f9ec, 52 bytes): **established.**
  An encoder that forwards to `xdr_u_long` (global-table slot at 0x154fa00) with its own arguments: an element index is one unsigned long on the wire.
- `xdr_SDBfileOffset` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154fa20, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Also a one-call forward to `xdr_u_long` (0x154fa34): file offsets are unsigned longs.
- `xdr_SDBdataType` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154fce8, 52 bytes): **established.**
  Forwards to the enumeration encoder through a global slot (0x154fcfc), so a data type travels as an XDR enumeration.
- `xdr_SDBversionNumber` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154fe54, 104 bytes): **established.**
  Encodes the version as two unsigned shorts, the first at the pointer and the second at pointer+2 (`xdr_u_short` calls at 0x154fe74 and 0x154fe90), failing with 0 if either fails and returning 1 otherwise.
- `xdr_SDBsymbolName` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154fd1c, 112 bytes): **established.**
  Encodes a symbol name as an XDR string with a limit of 31 characters (`xdr_string` with 0x1f at 0x154fd44) and then writes a terminating zero at index 31 (0x154fd60); a null pointer, or a name pointer holding null, returns 0.
- `vfmtFindKonaFormatFile` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154b5e0, 76 bytes): **established.**
  Looks a format file up in the directory `/usr/gfx/ucode/KONA/dg4/vfo` (the string behind the small-data pointer at 0x154b608) by passing the caller's two arguments and that path to the shared finder `vfmtFindFormatFile` (0x154b390).
- `vfmtFindOdyFormatFile` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154b6d0, 76 bytes): **established.**
  The VPro form of the same call: the directory argument is `/usr/gfx/ucode/ODSY/vof` (the pointer loaded at 0x154b6f8) and the lookup is `vfmtFindFormatFile`.
- `vfmtFindSG2FormatFile` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154b720, 76 bytes): **established.**
  The same wrapper for the older board whose formats live in `/var/X11/Xvc/SG2_formats` (pointer loaded at 0x154b748).
- `GenlockNotify..OF` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x17034d0, 60 bytes): **established.**
  Tells the video-control extension that the lock state changed: it loads the word at +0 of the record at +0x10 of its first argument and calls `XvcSendScreenLockChangeEvent` with that and its third argument (0x17034e8 to 0x17034f4).
- `sgiReleaseCapabilityUnsupported` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x167db90, 28 bytes): **established.**
  Refuses to release an optional capability: it returns the value of `nmbxErrorBase` plus one (0x167db9c to 0x167dba8), the extension's error code for "not supported", and reads nothing else.
- `sgiEnableCapabilityUnsupported` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16ffbc0, 28 bytes): **established.**
  The enable counterpart, with the same body: `nmbxErrorBase` plus one is returned (0x16ffbcc to 0x16ffbd8).

## Audio-library configuration, port and sample routines

The audio library's objects carry a magic number in their first word: 0xbb82 for a configuration record and 0xbb81 for a port. The accessors below check it, and on a bad or null object they set `errno` through `setoserror` (2 for a bad configuration, 1 for a bad port, 21 for a sample count that is not a whole number of frames), call the library's error hook (the variable `_AL2error`) when one is installed, and return -1 or 0.

- `alNewConfig` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1551960, 164 bytes): **established.**
  Allocates a 0x28-byte configuration with `malloc`, copies the library's default configuration into it with `bcopy` and stamps the magic 0xbb82 into its first word (0x1551960 to 0x15519b0). On allocation failure it sets `errno` 6 and calls the error hook with the out-of-memory message, returning null.
- `alFreeConfig` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1551a10, 164 bytes): **established.**
  Frees a configuration after checking the magic 0xbb82 and returns 0; otherwise `errno` 2, the error hook with the bad-configuration text, and -1.
- `alGetChannels` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15521f0, 152 bytes): **established.**
  Returns the channel count held at +0x10 of a configuration whose magic is 0xbb82; the failure path is the common one.
- `alGetSampFmt` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15529e0, 152 bytes): **established.**
  Returns the sample-format word at +0xc of a checked configuration.
- `alGetWidth` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15624c0, 152 bytes): **established.**
  Returns the sample width word at +8 of a configuration whose first word is the magic 0xbb82 (the compare at 0x15624ec, the load at 0x1562540). A null handle or a wrong magic instead sets `errno` 2 through `setoserror` (0x15624f4), calls the error hook when one is set with the invalid-handle message, the routine's name and the handle (0x1562520), and returns -1 (0x156252c).
- `alGetFloatMax` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1562420, 152 bytes): **established.**
  Returns the double at +0x18 of a checked configuration (the maximum used for floating-point samples), and 0 on a bad object.
- `alGetFD` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1552290, 148 bytes): **established.**
  For a port (magic 0xbb81) returns the file descriptor at +0x24; a bad port gives `errno` 1 and -1.
- `ALgetqueuesize` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154fee0, 188 bytes): **established.**
  Returns the queue size in sample frames held at +4 of a checked configuration, multiplied by the channel count at +0x10 when bit 30 of the flags word at +0x24 is set (the `sll` by 1 test at 0x154ff10), i.e. in samples instead of frames for such configurations.
- `ALreadsamps` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15500c0, 148 bytes): **established.**
  Reads samples as frames: the count is shifted right by the port's frame-shift word at +0x7c and rejected with `errno` 21 unless shifting back gives the original count; otherwise it calls `alReadFrames` with the frame count.
- `ALwritesamps` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1550160, 144 bytes): **established.**
  The write twin: the same shift-and-verify test on the count (with the +0x7c shift word, 0x1550160 to 0x1550194), `errno` 21 on a remainder, otherwise a call to `alWriteFrames`.
- `ALgetfillpoint` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15502a0, 176 bytes): **established.**
  Returns the port's fill point in sample units: the ring size at +8 minus the word behind the pointer at +0x18, shifted left by the word at +0x7c and right by the word at +0x80 (a port has magic 0xbb81).
- `ALsetfillpoint` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1550350, 180 bytes): **established.**
  The inverse: converts the argument from sample units (shift left by +0x80, right by +0x7c), subtracts it from the ring size at +8 and stores the result through the pointer at +0x18, then returns 0.
- `_AL2WriteMonoToStereo_24` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1553a20, 188 bytes): **established.**
  Duplicates each 32-bit mono sample into two adjacent words (left and right) for the count in the third argument, handling an odd count first with one pair and then two samples per loop pass (the unrolled loop at 0x1553a58 onward); a count of zero or below does nothing.
- `_AL2Read4ChanFromStereo_24` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1558c30, 140 bytes): **established.**
  Widens stereo 32-bit input to four-channel output: each input pair is stored unchanged as the first two words of a four-word frame and the last two words are zeroed; the loop is unrolled by two with the tail handled after the loop.
- `SDBcloseReadDatabase` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154f318, 184 bytes): **established.**
  Releases a read-mode symbol database: it frees the entry array at +0xac and the I/O object at +0 (calling the method at +0x1c of that object's table first), through the memory hooks of `_SDBmemface` (the free entry at +0xc), closes the stream at +4 with `fclose` unless it is standard input, and finally frees the database record.
- `xdr_SDBsymbolTableEntry` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154fd8c, 200 bytes): **established.**
  Encodes one 0x2c-byte symbol entry as the name (at +0xc), the data type (+0), the element index (+4) and the file offset (+8), calling the four `xdr_SDB` encoders in that order and returning 1 only if all four succeed.
- `vfmtFindCmpFormatFile` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154b630, 156 bytes): **established.**
  Searches two directories in turn through `vfmtFindFormatFile`: `/usr/gfx/ucode/VOYAGER/vfo` first (0x154b658) and, when that call does not return 0, `/usr/gfx/ucode/STINGRAY/vfo` (0x154b694), returning the second result; when the first returns 0 the result is 0.
- `vfmtGetLastKonaFileStat` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x154bb20, 132 bytes): **established.**
  Copies the 152-byte (19 doubleword, count 0x13 at 0x154bb30) file-status record saved by the last format-file lookup, from a static buffer at +0x21c8 of the data block, into the caller's buffer and returns 1.

## Raster-operation word loops and small drawing helpers

The `nfb<N>RopLoop` routines combine a source run into a destination run one 32-bit word at a time. They take the source pointer, the destination pointer and the destination end pointer, and do nothing when the destination is already at or past the end. N is the X graphics-function number (0 clear, 1 AND, 2 AND-reverse, 3 copy, 4 AND-inverted, 5 no-op, 6 XOR, 7 OR, 8 NOR, 9 equivalence, 10 invert, 11 OR-reverse, 12 copy-inverted, 13 OR-inverted, 14 NAND, 15 set). All sixteen exist in the dynamic table; 5 is the empty routine read above, 0, 10 and 15 store constants or the complement and are longer.

- `nfb1RopLoop..BH` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15fe1e0, 184 bytes): **established.**
  Destination = source AND destination. A head loop first handles (words >> 2) & 3 single words with prefetches 0x100 bytes ahead on the destination and 0xfc on the source, then the main loop does four words a pass while the destination is below the end.
- `nfb2RopLoop..CH` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1655e70, 200 bytes): **established.**
  Destination = source AND NOT destination, in the same head-then-four-word-pass shape as the AND loop; the extra inversions make it 16 bytes longer.
- `nfb3RopLoop..DH` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16bace0, 184 bytes): **established.**
  The plain word copy, and the only loop of the family that is unrolled to eight words (32 bytes) a pass, with a head loop for (words >> 2) & 7 words (0x16bace0 to 0x16bad98).
- `nfb4RopLoop..EH` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15d2950, 200 bytes): **established.**
  Clears in the destination every bit that is set in the source (destination AND NOT source), with the four-word main loop and a head of up to three words.
- `nfb6RopLoop..GH` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x169d300, 184 bytes): **established.**
  Exclusive-or of source into destination, four words a pass after a head of up to three words.
- `nfb7RopLoop..HH` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1702160, 184 bytes): **established.**
  Inclusive-or of source into destination with the family's usual head loop and four-word main loop.
- `nfb8RopLoop..IH` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15a0b60, 184 bytes): **established.**
  Stores NOT (source OR destination), i.e. the NOR of the two runs (0x15a0b60 to 0x15a0c18); shape as the others, with four words a pass.
- `nfb9RopLoop..JH` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1632ff0, 200 bytes): **established.**
  Equivalence: destination = destination XOR NOT source, over the same two-phase loop.
- `nfb11RopLoop..LH` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16749b0, 200 bytes): **established.**
  Destination = source OR NOT destination (OR-reverse), head loop plus four-word pass.
- `nfb13RopLoop..NH` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1568160, 200 bytes): **established.**
  Destination = destination OR NOT source (OR-inverted); the head loop covers up to three words and the main loop four.
- `nfb14RopLoop..OH` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x160c5f0, 200 bytes): **established.**
  NAND: the stored word is NOT (source AND destination), looped like the rest with a four-word main pass.
- `miFreeArcs..KO` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1563220, 188 bytes): **established.**
  Releases the cached arc data. Starting at record 1 when the top two bits of the context word at +0x10 equal 2 and at record 0 otherwise, it walks the 0x18-byte records downward; each record that has a positive count at +0, +8 or +0x10 frees the block pointer next to it (+4, +0xc, +0x14); then the array itself is freed (0x1563240 to 0x1563290).
- `miFreeArcCache` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1617b40, 136 bytes): **established.**
  Empties a static cache of 25 entries of 16 bytes (from 0x173f558 to 0x173f6e8): for each entry holding a block pointer at +0xc it zeroes the word at +0 and the halfword at +4, frees the block and clears the pointer (0x1617b7c to 0x1617b9c). It clears a stored cursor at 0x173f6e8, and clears a second flag at 0x173f6ec when its second argument is nonzero (`movn` at 0x1617b74).
- `cfb32CopyArea` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1573be0, 156 bytes): **established.**
  Jumps through a pointer slot to `cfb32BitBlt`; the 32-bit layer's area copy is a thin entry over that block-transfer routine and keeps no state of its own.
- `cfbCopyArea` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16daaa0, 164 bytes): **established.**
  Same shape for the 8-bit layer, calling `cfbBitBlt`.
- `cfb16CopyArea` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1709690, 160 bytes): **established.**
  The 16-bit layer's area copy calls `cfb16BitBlt` with the arguments it was given.
- `nfbTiledFillRects` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x157d250, 148 bytes): **established.**
  Fills rectangles with the context's tile through the window's own operation table. It takes the tile at +0x20 of the context and the context's private record (`nfbGCPrivateIndex`), and calls the window-private table's entry at +0x1c with the rectangle list, the tile data (+0x20) and pitch (+0x1c), tile width and height (+0xc, +0xe), the private block at +0x58, its flag byte at +0x48 and word at +0x4c, and the drawable (0x157d250 to 0x157d2e0).
- `cfb32FillRectTileOdd` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1627e80, 140 bytes): **established.**
  Picks the box-fill routine by the context: with plane mask -1 (+0x14) and raster op 3 (byte +5) it uses `cfb32FillBoxTile32sCopy`, otherwise `cfb32FillBoxTile32sGeneral`, and passes the tile data (+0x20), the tile origin (the drawable's x, y halfwords at +8, +0xa plus the context's origin halfwords at +0x28, +0x2a), the raster op and the plane mask.
- `miClearDrawable` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1587a90, 192 bytes): **established.**
  Paints the whole drawable in the context's background: it saves the foreground (+0x18) and background (+0x1c), makes the background the foreground with `DoChangeGC` (mask 4), validates the context, calls its polygon-fill-rectangle entry (+0x2c of the table at +0x48) with the one rectangle (0, 0, drawable width at +0xc, height at +0xe), then restores the foreground and validates again.
- `miBSDestroyWindow..JCB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15d76a0, 124 bytes): **established.**
  Backing-store wrapper for window destruction. It takes the original destroy-window procedure from the wrapper's screen-private record (entry +0x14, through a module-private index), puts it back in the screen's slot at +0xb8, calls it for the window, frees the window's backing store with `miBSFree`, and finally reinstalls itself at +0xb8; the result is the original procedure's.
- `miImageText8` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1669f20, 140 bytes): **established.**
  Draws 8-bit image text: it converts the string to glyph records with `GetGlyphs` (font pointer from the context at +0x2c, mode 0) into a 1024-byte stack buffer, and when at least one glyph came back calls the context's image-glyph entry (+0x44 of the table at +0x48) with the drawable, context, position, glyph count, buffer and a zero.
- `miImageText16` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15f9220, 164 bytes): **established.**
  The 16-bit version: the same `GetGlyphs` and +0x44 glyph call, with mode 2 when the font's halfword at +0xa (the row-versus-column flag) is zero and mode 3 otherwise (`movn` at 0x15f9280).
- `RecomputeExposures..NG` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x162f580, 184 bytes): **established.**
  Rebuilds a window's exposure regions. With no exposure record at +0x44 it returns 3 at once (0x162f580 and the branch at 0x162f5a8). Otherwise it calls the screen's region entry at +0x16c three times: for the record's first region from the window's region at +0x2c and the second argument, for the region 0xc bytes on from the regions at +0x38 and +0x48, and for that same region again with the second argument; it returns 1.
- `miRecomputeExposures..PAB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x163cbc0, 184 bytes): **established.**
  The same instruction sequence as `RecomputeExposures..NG`, reached under another name (only the `gp` set-up constants at 0x163cbd0 and the early-exit branch at 0x163cbe8 differ): the +0x44 record check with result 3, then three region calls through the screen entry +0x16c, result 1.
- `hookEllipseY..KP` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16327f0, 152 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end; the floating-point condition codes were traced by hand through both sign cases).
  Inverts a curve in the ellipse code (arguments: x in the first float register, a pointer to two doubles, the shape record, a flag; result a double). It forms x times the double at +0x18 of the shape record. When the double at +0x28 is nonzero it divides by it and returns the cube root of the quotient (`cbrt`, 0x1632858), or the negated cube root of the negated quotient when the quotient is negative (0x1632864 to 0x1632870). When that divisor is zero it returns one of the two doubles at the pointer: the first for x above zero with the flag clear or x below zero with the flag set, the second for x equal to zero and for the other two combinations (0x1632814 to 0x1632880).
- `rrmDestroyWindow` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1641f20, 132 bytes): **established.**
  Resource-manager wrapper for window destruction. It finds the screen's resource-manager record, calls that record's entry at +0x64 first, then temporarily puts the saved original destroy procedure (+0x74 of the record) into the screen's slot at +0xb8, calls it for the window, and reinstalls itself there (0x1641f20 to 0x1641f98).
- `miTileVirtualBS..KFB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16557f0, 176 bytes): **established.**
  Switches a window's background to the state its attributes ask for. In the window's backing-store record (+0x74) it first releases the old tile pixmap (screen entry +0xf0) when the old kind byte at +0x16 is 3, takes the new kind from the top two bits of the window word at +0x7c, stores the new tile pixmap pointer (+0x6c) at +0x18 and bumps its reference count (+0x18) when the kind is 3, sets byte +0x15 to 2 unless it is already 3, and creates a backing-store pixmap when the kind is 1.
- `SwapDone..MF` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1659f90, 148 bytes): **established.**
  Completion notice for a buffer swap: for a window that has a resource-manager record it clears bit 4 of the halfword at +0xa8 of that window's 0x24-byte clip-id entry (found by the record's index at +0x14), and then reports the swap complete to the double-buffer code with `dbcDelayedSwapBufferDone`, passing the window id at +4.
- `SwapInitiated..NF` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16aea00, 136 bytes): **established.**
  Start notice for a swap: when bit 1 of the third argument is set and the window has a record, it clears or sets bit 2 of the record's word at +0x20 according to bit 2 of the argument, and calls `dbcNoticeSwapInitiated` with that bit as the second argument.
- `nfbReadDisplayShutdown..IJ` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x169c230, 128 bytes): **established.**
  Tears down the screen's read-display state: when the record under `nfbRDScreenPrivateIndex` has a shutdown procedure at +4 it calls it with the screen, then the screen-private slot is cleared either way (0x169c230 to 0x169c2a8).
- `nfbHWColormapScreenInit` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1679410, 200 bytes): **established.**
  Copies a screen's hardware colour-map description into private storage. It allocates n times 0x1c bytes (n is the second argument) and copies the caller's entry table into it at +0x74 of the record, allocates 0x228 bytes and copies the caller's second table into it at +0x7c, then stores 0x17 at +0x78 and n at +0x70; if the second allocation fails it frees the first and returns 0 (0x1679410 to 0x16794d0).
- `nfbPostValidateTree` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x167ce30, 200 bytes): **established.**
  Runs after a clip-tree validation. In mode 3 it subtracts from each of the word array at +0x40 of the screen's record the matching word of the array at +0x44 (as many as the count at +0x28) and zeroes the subtrahend; in any other mode it calls the record's entry at +0x6c with the screen when bit 3 of the window's frame-buffer-layer flags (+0x20) is set. A null window does nothing.
- `miBSCreateGC..ICB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x168af20, 156 bytes): **established.**
  Backing-store wrapper for context creation. It calls the saved original create-context procedure (entry +0x10 of the wrapper's screen record) with its own slot at +0x130 of the screen temporarily replaced, and if that succeeded it stores the context's operation table (+0x44) into the context's private slot and points +0x44 at the wrapper's table at 0x1738da0; either way it reinstalls itself at +0x130.
- `miBSDestroyGC..FFB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x169c2b0, 168 bytes): **established.**
  Undoes that wrapper: it copies the saved operation-table pointers from the context's private record (words 5 and 4) back to +0x44 and +0x48, frees the record's graphics context when its first word is set, calls the restored destroy entry (+0xc), resets +0x44 and +0x48 to the wrapper's default tables (0x1738d28 and 0x1738d48), and frees the private record.
- `miDestroyGC` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16a6c10, 172 bytes): **established.**
  Releases what a context's private record holds: a pixmap pointer at word 1 (screen entry +0xf0), a clip region at word 2 when bit 6 of the flags word 0 is set (screen entry +0x15c), and the block at +0x50 of the record at +0x48, which goes to `Xfree` when nonzero.
- `DelayValidateClipCallback..DF` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16b0e90, 176 bytes): **established.**
  Timer callback that finishes a postponed clip validation: from the request record (window, flag) it clears word +0x18 of the window's GLX private, runs `ValidateClip` for the window, frees the request record and the timer with `TimerFree`, and zeroes words +0x44 and +0x48 of the window's resource-manager record.
- `nfbDestroyClip..NJ` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16b2b30, 152 bytes): **established.**
  Drops a context's clip. The clip kind is bits 19:18 of the word at +0x10: kind 1 (a pixmap) calls the screen's entry +0xf0, any other nonzero kind calls +0x15c with the clip pointer at +0x38, and then the pointer and the two kind bits are cleared; kind 0 does nothing.
- `miBSChangeWindowAttributes..HCB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16ba960, 196 bytes): **established.**
  Backing-store wrapper for attribute changes. It runs the saved original procedure (entry +0xc of the wrapper's screen record) with the screen's slot at +0xc0 temporarily pointing at it, and when that succeeded and bit 0x40 of the change mask is set it frees the window's backing store if the backing-store kind (bits 27:26 of +0x7c) is 0 and bit 25 is clear, or allocates it otherwise; it then reinstalls itself at +0xc0.
- `miPolySegment` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16cab20, 152 bytes): **established.**
  Draws each segment as its own two-point polyline: for every 8-byte segment of the list it calls entry +0x18 of the context's table (+0x48) with the drawable, the context, coordinate mode 0, a count of 2 and a pointer to the segment, re-reading the table pointer each time (0x16cab40 to 0x16cab90).
- `rrmValidateTree` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16dbfe0, 176 bytes): **established.**
  Wraps window-tree validation: a window that has a resource-manager record first goes through `rrmInvalidateWindowTree`; then the saved original procedure (+0x7c of the screen's resource-manager record) is placed in the screen's slot at +0xcc, called with the three arguments, and the wrapper reinstalls itself.
- `miBSCloseScreen..ECB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16dc270, 148 bytes): **established.**
  Unhooks the backing-store layer at screen close: it restores five saved procedure pointers from the layer's screen record into the screen at +0x98, +0xa4, +0xa8, +0xc0 and +0x130, frees the record, and calls the restored close procedure (+0x98) with the original arguments.
- `odsyFreeFmtInfoArray..GB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16e21a0, 192 bytes): **established.**
  Frees an array of 0x138-byte format records: for each record whose flag at +0x12c and pointer at +0x130 are both nonzero it frees the pointer and clears it, and then the array itself is freed when non-null (0x16e21a0 to 0x16e2250).
- `miDestroyClip` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x170d3d0, 152 bytes): **established.**
  The machine-independent twin of `nfbDestroyClip..NJ` with the same instruction sequence: clip kind in bits 19:18 of the word at +0x10, screen entry +0xf0 for a pixmap clip and +0x15c for a region clip, then pointer at +0x38 and the kind bits cleared.
- `miMarkUnrealizedWindow` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x170fe50, 124 bytes): **established.**
  Unless the window is the stop window and the flag is zero, it applies the screen's region entry +0x188 to the window's two regions (+0x2c and +0x38), and between them calls the screen's entry +0xe8 with the window when one is installed.
- `nfbMarkWindow` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x17120d0, 176 bytes): **established.**
  Flags a window as changed: it sets to 1 the word indexed by the window's number (+0x18 of its frame-buffer-layer record) in the array at +0x3c of the screen's record, and when the window has no saved-extent record yet it allocates 0x18 bytes (with the `Must_have_memory` flag raised around the call), stores the window's x and y halfwords (+8, +0xa) at +0 and +2, zeroes words +4 and +8 and links the block at window +0x44.
- `rrmInitRootWindow..AG` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x171d9a0, 168 bytes): **established.**
  Publishes the board number on the root window. It interns the atom `_SGI_RRM_BOARD_NUM` (18 characters) with `MakeAtom` and on failure prints "Couldn't intern _SGI_RRM_BOARD_NUM atom" with `ErrorF`; otherwise it takes the byte at +0xc of the record at +0x44 from `ddxGetScreenInfo` and writes it with `ChangeWindowProperty` (format 32, replace, one item) to the root window.

## The three constants built after `sqrt` in `odsyInitDefaultHW..N`

- `odsyInitDefaultHW..N` three stored constants (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, calls at 0x15c67e0, 0x15c6804 and 0x15c6820, conversions at 0x15c67e8, 0x15c680c and 0x15c6844): **inferred.**
  The module was compiled without a prototype for `sqrt`, so the compiler treats its result as an integer in v0 and converts that with `mtc1`/`cvt.s.W` (the first two, stored at region +0x244c and +0x2454) and `cvt.d.W` then times 0.25 (the third, stored at +0x2458), while the real result is the double in f0. The call goes straight to the C maths library: the module's global-offset slot for `sqrt` (0x1733a44) is pre-bound to 0x1129140, which is the entry `__sqrt` at 0x409140 of `usr/lib32/mips4/libm.so` plus that library's quickstart base 0xd20000 (the R10000 build of the library; the generic `usr/lib32/libm.so` has `__sqrt` at 0x409104 instead), and the symbol has no lazy-binding stub (its dynamic-symbol value is 0), so the dynamic linker resolves it at load time and never runs between the three calls. `__sqrt` at 0x409140 is a leaf that tests the argument with `dmfc1`, executes `sqrt.d` into f0 and returns, using only t0, t1, t3, at, a0, gp and ra, and the libm routine of the mips3 and generic builds is the same code. v0 is written once in `odsyInitDefaultHW..N` before the calls, by `lui v0,0x2000` at 0x15c66d8, and the listing between that and 0x15c6844 never writes it, so v0 is 0x20000000 at all three conversions. The words at region +0x244c and +0x2454 therefore hold the single-precision value 536870912.0 (0x4e000000) and the word at +0x2458 holds 134217728.0 (0x4d000000), not the square roots 2.83, 1.41 and 0.35 that the arguments 8.0, 2.0 and 2.0 (the last result scaled by 0.25) suggest. The module side is read in its listing: v0 is loaded once at 0x15c66d8 and never written before the conversions, the slot is a global `sqrt` with dynamic-symbol value 0 and file value 0x1129140, and the argument constants are 8.0, 2.0 and 2.0. The library side, that `__sqrt` leaves v0 alone, is cited from that library's entry at 0x409140; the `usr/lib32/mips4/libm.so` that holds it is not in this workspace's install tree, and the public libc `sqrt.s` shows the same shape (`_sqrt_d` is a leaf that writes only f0). The routine was not run under an emulator.

## Clip-identifier stealing, buffer swap, plane clears and PIO image upload

The resource manager keeps a per-screen table of clip identifiers (the record at screen-private index `rrmScreenPrivateIndex`): word 0 is the number of identifiers, the entries are 0x24 bytes each from +0x98 (+0x98 and +0x9c first and last window of the entry, +0xa0 and +0xa4 two mask words, halfword +0xa8 flags, halfword +0xaa the visual class, +0xac a use count), and word +0x84 is a round-robin cursor.

- `StealID` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1563dc0, 5044 bytes): **established.**
  Finds a display identifier for a window when none is free by merging or re-using entries (argument: the window; it keeps a round-robin cursor at +0x84 of the screen's resource-manager record). The whole listing was read, 1254 instructions up to the final branch at 0x156516c (delay slot 0x1565170) (the dynamic symbol is 5044 bytes, which runs past the code into the next routine). In the identifier entries (0x24 bytes from +0x98: head window +0x98, mode words +0xa0 and +0xa4, flag halfword +0xa8, class halfword +0xaa), flag bit 2 means two or more windows share the entry, bit 4 is the bit `AssignID..FG` clears when the list empties and bit 0 marks a reserved entry. Two entries are compatible when the head window's mode words (+0x20, +0x24) and the other entry's (+0xa0, +0xa4) are equal after ANDing with the screen's masks (+0x10, +0x14). The routine has three scans.
  The first (0x1563e60 to 0x1563f4c) takes an entry of the window's class with flag bits 2 and 4 both clear (one window) whose head window has no pending swap (bit 3 of record byte +4 clear), and looks for a second entry of the same class that is compatible with it. When the second entry is shared (flag bit 2) the single head window is flushed through the screen procedure +0x20 (window, 0, 0) when its record byte +6 has bit 3 and the screen's word +4 is set (0x1564710); when it is not shared but its head window also has no pending swap, both heads are flushed with +0x20 (the second call passes 1, 0x15649a8). In both cases the head window is unlinked with the standard list-removal block (clearing bit 3 of its flags and setting its identifier to -1) and given the second entry with `AssignID..FG`, and the requesting window is unlinked from its old entry and given the freed one (tail calls at 0x1564970, 0x15649a0, 0x1564bfc and 0x1564c28).
  The second scan (0x1563f60 to 0x1564124) starts at the cursor plus one, wrapping at the identifier count, for at most one lap: a single-window entry of the right class is flushed (+0x20) and every other entry is tested for compatibility; a compatible shared entry takes the window at once (0x1564590), otherwise the last compatible single-window entry with no pending swap is chosen and `NewID..GG` moves the head window into it, after which the requester is unlinked and assigned the vacated entry with `AssignID..FG` and the cursor is stored (0x156424c). When no compatible entry exists a third scan (0x1564270 to 0x1564f34) accepts shared entries and entries whose first mode word differs only in bit 2 (the mask -5 at 0x1564390); after flushing, an entry whose window class and masked words agree with the window's is re-programmed with the screen procedure +0x28 (window, record, screen), has flag bit 3 set and takes the window's mode words into +0xa0 and +0xa4 (0x1565080 to 0x15650b0).
  When all three fail the last resort (0x15644ec) advances the cursor to the next entry of the window's class that is not reserved (bit 0 clear), takes the colour-map identifier of its head window from `cmapDID`, merges the two identifier lists with `mergeDIDLists..EG` and gives the window the entry with `NewID..GG`, storing the cursor. Open sub-fact: unknown which entry shape the third scan prefers when a shared entry and a bit-2-only difference both exist, since the scan's two loops each choose the last match.
- `odsyInitiateBufferSwap` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16562f0, 4024 bytes): **established.**
  Starts a buffer swap for a list of windows (array of window pointers, count). For each window it finds the resource-manager record (an error message through `ErrorF` when it is missing), asks the record's procedure at +0x20 to finish a pending change when bit 3 of byte +4 is set, and sets or clears bit 2 of word +0x20 and bit 30 of word +0x24 according to byte +2 of the double-buffer private (1 selects the cleared form). A window that is viewable (bit 11 of window word +0x7c) needs a clip identifier for its new buffer state: it scans the identifiers from the top for a reusable one flagged 0x10, unlinks the window from its old identifier, calls `AssignID` or, when none fits, `StealID`, and stores the window's mask word +0x24 into the board record's table at +0x58 (12 bytes an identifier, word +8). A window that is not viewable is reported done at once with `dbcDelayedSwapBufferDone`. When any window was queued it bumps the 16-bit swap serial at region +0x2b3c, sends the settle sequences and block 0x10a5a with the word at region +0x2b78 ORed with 0x8000 and the serial, then for each queued window (last first) re-sends the 0x11017 descriptor in its four variants (as held, third word zero, bit 23 of the third word set, as held) bracketed by settle sequences, and issues the swap request, ioctl 1020 (`RRM_MGR_SwapBuf`) on the descriptor at +100 of the window's screen-private record, with five words, in memory order record +0xc, record +0x14, the window id, record +0x24 and record +0x20 (the stores at 0x1657158 to 0x1657178); on failure it prints the error with `perror` and reports the swap done, on success it copies record words +0x20 and +0x24 into the identifier's +0xa0 and +0xa4. The result is 1 when nothing was queued or a request failed and 0 when the last request succeeded.
- `odsyClearPlanes` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16a6cc0, 5692 bytes): **established.**
  Clears a list of rectangles (a region: count at +4, 8-byte boxes from +8; one rectangle when it has no list) in one buffer plane, selected by its third argument, with the value in the fourth. It first refreshes the window descriptor (block 0x1204b with the origin and extent words and the three 0xffffff/0 pairs at region +0x2b40 to +0x2b5c) when the clip box changed, sets the destination word at region +0x84 (0x10404) and then branches. Argument 0 (the colour planes): destination 0x100000, plane selectors +0x2664 = 0 and +0x2668 = 2 (words 0x22008000 and 0x23008002), a three-operand block 0xc580cc08 with the colour's red, green and blue bytes, enable words 0xff0ff0 at +0xe8 and +0xec (block 0x11023), mask word +0xc8 = 3 (0x10422) and the write mask at +0x2b68 = 0xf for a board whose word 4 is 2, else 3 (0x10424). Argument 1000 (inferred to be the depth plane): destination 0x80000, selectors 1 and 3 (0x22008001, 0x23008003), a zero block 0xce80cc08 and the value as one full word in block 0xc580c406, enable words 0xff000 and 1, mask 0xf. Any other value (inferred to be small-field planes): destination 0x80000, a write mask of 0xc with the value 1 sent whole and an 0xff field mask for argument 1, otherwise a 5-bit field (value shifted left by 3, field mask 0xf8) when the value is below 0x20 or a 3-bit field (value and 7, field mask 7). Every branch then rebuilds and sends the 0x11017 descriptor in its four settle-bracketed forms and sends block 0x14400 with operand 7, and each rectangle goes out as four vertex words 0x8080c800 with the corners (x0,y0), (x0,y1), (x1,y1), (x1,y0), followed by 0x14001; the pending-settle flag at +0x1cb8 is set to 1 and the board record's flag at +0x68 is left 1 on return.
- `odsyDrawImagePIO..G` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x167dbb0, 10044 bytes, with the out-of-line tail from 0x167f2e0 to 0x16802ec): **established.**
  Uploads an image rectangle (shorts x0, y0, x1, y1, the pixel data, a row stride, a raster-operation code and the window) by writing the pixels through the command FIFO. It selects a write mask from the window's double-buffer byte (-2 or 0 gives 3, -3 or 1 gives 0xc, -1 gives 0xf or 3 by the board's word 4, tile case 7 gives 0xc), refreshes the window descriptor 0x1204b when the clip box changed, sets bits 11:10 of the state word at +0x88 to 1 (block 0x10405), sends the write mask (0x10424) and the code (0x10422), builds and sends the 0x11017 descriptor from board words 1 or 2 with fields from the window's pixel size and type, then block 0x11453 with five words at +0x1b34, the shadow selector at +0x2680 set to 0x12f (sent as 0x29008000 form), block 0x14400 with operand 10, and the rectangle as floats in block 0xcf80a92f (eleven words) followed by a quad header 0x8080c800. The image rows go out through transfer-state word 0x4570 (operand 0x71) and data bursts led by a word that holds the burst length times 0x400 and 0x14000; rows whose start or width is not word-aligned are first copied byte by byte, or with `bcopy`, into an aligned stack buffer, and the loop works in bursts of at most 16 words (8 for the 8-bit-per-component case selected by format 0x12). It ends with 0x14001, restores the shadow selector, resends 0x11453 with zeroed words and clears bits 11:10 of +0x88 again.
  The whole function was read in a listing of 2509 instructions (the first 5936 bytes from the Ghidra listing and the out-of-line tail from the objdump), with the constant stores to the command FIFO extracted in address order. The pixel mode is word +0x14 of the window's frame-buffer private record, the value `odsyValidateWindowPriv` stores there. A jump table of 12 words at 0x172abc8 (loaded at 0x167e298 to 0x167e2b8, bound check `sltiu` 12 at 0x167e2a0) selects the mode's set-up: modes 0, 1 and 7 share the code at 0x167f238, mode 2 is at 0x168004c, 3 at 0x167fecc, 4 at 0x167fd4c, 5 at 0x167fbc4, 6 at 0x167fa24, 8 at 0x167f8a8, 9 at 0x167f72c, 10 at 0x167f5b0 and 11 at 0x167f438; a mode of 12 or more prints "Unrecognized mode: 0x%x" (0x167e328). Every case ORs bit 20 (0x100000) into the state word at +0x84 (after a sync prefix when the word at +0x1cb8 is set), sends 0x10404 with it, caches the format words at +0x2664 and +0x2668 (sending 0x22008001 and 0x23008003 for modes 0, 1, 7 and 8 to 11, and 0x22008000 and 0x23008002 for modes 2 to 6) when they differ, sends 0xce80cc08 (0xc580cc09 for modes 5 and 6, 0xc580cc08 for modes 2 to 4) followed by three or four zero words and, for the first group, 0xc580c406 with a zero word, and finally resends the four-word 0x11023 descriptor from +0xe8 to +0xf4 of the board state when its two words changed. The field the case puts in the descriptor differs by mode: a shift of 12 (0xc) for modes 0, 1, 7, 10 and 11, of 16 (0x10) for modes 8 and 9, of 4 for mode 6, of 14 (0xe) for mode 5, of 19 (0x13) in mode 3 and of 20 (0x14) in mode 2, with the masks 0xfff, 0xc00, 0xff0, 0x800 and 0xf00 in the cases for modes 6, 5, 4, 3 and 2. The image format code is compared with 16 (0x10), 17 (0x11), 18 (0x12) and 20 (0x14) at 0x167e838 to 0x167e868: format 18 and the other three take different branches into the data loop. Open sub-fact: unknown what the stride-dependent fields of the transfer word encode; the stream shape is read, the field meanings are not tied to a board register.
- `nfb12RopLoop..MH` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16d9210, 216 bytes): **established.**
  Copy-inverted: each destination word becomes NOT of the source word. Like the plain copy it is unrolled eight words a pass (head of (words >> 2) & 7 singles, main loop while the destination is below the end).
- `nfb0RopLoop..AH` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x171d880, 280 bytes): **established.**
  Clear: stores zero over the destination run and never reads the source (its first argument is not used). The head loop writes up to seven words with a prefetch for write, then the main loop is software-pipelined sixteen words (64 bytes) a pass, two interleaved eight-word blocks between 0x171d8f0 and 0x171d944, with a tail of five stores after the loop.
- `nfb15RopLoop..PH` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x163c9a0, 296 bytes): **established.**
  Set: the same pipelined store loop as the clear routine, storing 0xffffffff instead of 0 and ignoring the source.

The `nfb<N>MaskRopLoop` routines take a fourth argument, a bit mask, and write `(result AND mask) OR (destination AND NOT mask)` where the result is the same operation as the unmasked loop of that number; each has the unmasked loop's shape (head of up to three words, main loop four words a pass) with the mask and its complement held in registers.

- `nfb1MaskRopLoop..BI` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x170fd50, 248 bytes): **established.**
  Masked AND: the bits selected by the mask take source AND destination.
- `nfb2MaskRopLoop..CI` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x164d960, 264 bytes): **established.**
  Masked AND-reverse: masked bits take source AND NOT destination.
- `nfb3MaskRopLoop..DI` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1710330, 232 bytes): **established.**
  Masked copy, the cheapest of the family: `(source AND mask) OR (destination AND NOT mask)` (0x1710330 onward).
- `nfb4MaskRopLoop..EI` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1654f20, 264 bytes): **established.**
  Masked AND-inverted: masked bits take destination AND NOT source.
- `nfb6MaskRopLoop..GI` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x165a030, 248 bytes): **established.**
  Masked exclusive-or: masked bits take source XOR destination.
- `nfb7MaskRopLoop..HI` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x171af10, 248 bytes): **established.**
  Masked inclusive-or: masked bits take source OR destination.
- `nfb8MaskRopLoop..II` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x165eda0, 248 bytes): **established.**
  Masked NOR: masked bits take NOT (source OR destination).
- `nfb9MaskRopLoop..JI` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1718c00, 264 bytes): **established.**
  Masked equivalence: masked bits take destination XOR NOT source.
- `nfb11MaskRopLoop..LI` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x157f9a0, 264 bytes): **established.**
  Masked OR-reverse: masked bits take source OR NOT destination.
- `nfb12MaskRopLoop..MI` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1678680, 248 bytes): **established.**
  Masked copy-inverted: masked bits take NOT source, the rest keep the destination.
- `nfb13MaskRopLoop..NI` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x157fbe0, 264 bytes): **established.**
  Masked OR-inverted: masked bits take destination OR NOT source.
- `nfb14MaskRopLoop..OI` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1675e50, 264 bytes): **established.**
  Masked NAND: masked bits take NOT (source AND destination).

## Screen, window and context teardown and clip copy

- `nfbCloseScreen` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15f8540, 224 bytes): **established.**
  Releases the frame-buffer layer's screen record (the block at +0x74 of the screen): it calls the screen's region-destroy entry (+0x160) on each of the record's regions (count at +0x28, regions 0xc bytes apart from +0x2c), then frees the arrays at +0x2c, +0x3c, +0x40 and +0x44, the block at +0xa4 when set, the blocks at +0x5c and +0x60, and finally the record itself (0x15f8540 to 0x15f8610).
- `odsyCloseScreen` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x156e570, 288 bytes): **established.**
  Closes the board's screen state. It frees the block at +0x74 of the board record (clearing +0x74 and +0x78), destroys the two regions at +0x40 and +0x40+0xc through the screen entry +0x160 and frees that block, frees the block at +0x4c and the record, empties the module's cache of graphics-context operation tables (pointers cleared one by one from `odsyGCOpsCache` up to 0x1739348), calls `odsyDetachXvc` for the screen and ends by calling `nfbCloseScreen`.
- `nfbDestroyWindow` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16b6300, 212 bytes): **established.**
  Frees a window's two region arrays: for each of the screen record's regions (count at +0x28 of the record at screen +0x74) it destroys the matching 0xc-byte region of the arrays at +0x28 and +0x2c of the window's frame-buffer-layer record through the screen entry +0x160, then frees both arrays with `Xfree`.
- `nfbDestroyGC..OJ` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16f6950, 256 bytes): **established.**
  Releases a context's private record: the clip region at word 2 when bit 6 of word 0 is set (screen entry +0x15c), the pixmap at word 1 (+0xf0), regions at words 0x18 and 0x19, the block at +0x50 of the operation table at +0x48, and the block at +0x50 of the table that word 8 points to, each only when present.
- `nfbCopyClip..AK` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16302b0, 228 bytes): **established.**
  Copies the clip from a source context to the destination. A pixmap clip (kind 1 in bits 19:18 of +0x10) is shared by raising the pixmap's count at +0x18; a region clip is duplicated with the screen's create entry (+0x150) and copy entry (+0x158); the destination's own `nfbChangeClip` is then called with the kind and the clip pointer from +0x38.
- `miCopyClip` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x170d470, 244 bytes): **established.**
  The machine-independent counterpart with the same logic, but the final change goes through the destination context's operation table (entry +0x10 of the table at +0x44) instead of the frame-buffer-layer routine.
- `miChangeClip` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x167c020, 296 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the three clip kinds and the final flag and kind-bit updates were traced to addresses).
  Installs a new clip in a context after calling the context's saved destroy-clip entry (+0x14 of the table at +0x44). A pixmap clip (kind 1) is turned into a region with the screen entry +0x198 and the pixmap is released through +0xf0; a region clip (kind 2) is stored as given; any other nonzero kind is a rectangle list: +0x19c builds the region from the fourth argument (the count), the list and the kind (the ordering), and the list is freed with `Xfree`. The context then gets bit 19 of word +0x3c set (clip changed) and the kind bits 19:18 of +0x10 set to 2 when a region is held, 0 otherwise.
- `rrmDestroyDrawable` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x157fab0, 304 bytes): **established.**
  Releases a window's resource-manager record. If a delayed-validation timer is held (words at +0x44 and +0x48 of the record) it frees the block, frees the timer with `TimerFree` and clears both. A record whose flag bit 0 is clear is simply freed. Otherwise the clip identifier is given back through the screen's resource-manager entries: entry +0x44 (halfword +8 zero) or +0x3c (halfword +8 nonzero) for the window with the record's word at +0xc, then entry +0x60 with the screen, the window id and that word.
- `miCleanupSpanData` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1586ad0, 292 bytes): **established.**
  Flushes the two span groups of an opaque-stippled fill. When the context's fill style (top two bits of +0x10) is 2 it makes the foreground equal to the background for the first group (`DoChangeGC` mask 4 and `ValidateGC`, only when the two differ), fills the group at +0x14 of the span record with `miFillUniqueSpanGroup`, frees its span block at +0x1c if set, and restores the foreground. It always fills the group at +0 and frees its block at +8 when set.
- `miPostChangeSaveUnder` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x158f7c0, 292 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the parent test, the tree walk and the bit tests on +0x7c were traced, which corrected the child and sibling offsets).
  After a save-under change it asks the screen's attribute procedure (+0xc0) to re-evaluate backing store (mask 0x40) for the window's parent and, walking the window tree from the second argument by first-child (+0x24), next-sibling (+0x1c) and parent (+0x18) links, for every window that has a backing-store record at +0x74, backing-store kind 0 in bits 27:26 of +0x7c and bit 24 clear; a zero second argument ends the walk after the parent.
- `nfbPostChangeSaveUnder` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x161e420, 304 bytes): **established.**
  Walks the windows below the parent P of its first argument and calls one screen procedure for each window that has a backing-store record and no flags of the kind tested below. The arguments are S (a0) and W (a1); gp is 0x173a9c0 (set from t9 at 0x161e44c). The frame is 48 bytes: s2 at sp+0, s0 at sp+8, gp at sp+16, s1 at sp+24, s3 at sp+32 and ra at sp+40.
  Set-up. P is the word at S+24 (0x161e43c). When W is zero the walk start is zero (`beq` at 0x161e450 skips the test below). Otherwise the word at W+24 is XORed with P (0x161e458, 0x161e45c) and the start is S when that is nonzero, or W when W's parent is P (`movn` at 0x161e460). The procedure F is the word at +192 of the record at +16 of P (0x161e464, 0x161e478); it is not a GOT callee.
  Flag tests. For a window X, bit 24 of the word at X+124 is tested with `bltz` after a shift left by 7 (0x161e468, 0x161e474), and the two bits 27:26 with `andi` 3 after a shift right by 26 (0x161e46c, 0x161e470).
  P's own call. When bit 24 of P+124 is clear and bits 27:26 are zero (`beq` at 0x161e47c), a3 becomes the word at P+116 (0x161e530). If that is nonzero, F is called with a0 = P, a1 = 64 and a3 = P+116 (`jalr` at 0x161e540); a2 is not set on this path and is the value the caller passed. Otherwise P is not called. The walk starts in every case, unless the start is zero.
  Walk. For each window X from the start: when bit 24 of X+124 is clear, bits 27:26 are zero and X+116 is nonzero, F is called with a0 = X, a1 = 64 and a2 = X+116 (0x161e4dc to 0x161e4ec). a3 is the word at P+124 on the paths that skip P's call, and the word at P+116 on the path that made it. A window that fails a test gets no call. Either way the walk then goes to the first child (+36, 0x161e4b0), or when there is none to the next sibling (+28, 0x161e4fc). When the window has no next sibling the walk climbs to the parent (+24, 0x161e510) and takes that ancestor's next sibling (+28) if it has one. The walk ends when the climb reaches P (`beq` at 0x161e514), so it covers the subtree of the start and the later siblings of the start and its ancestors, below P.
  Return. v0 is the result of the last call made. With no call, v0 is (the word at W+24) XOR P when W is nonzero, and the entry value of v0 when W is zero. Nothing is stored except the frame.
- `miChangeSaveUnder` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1710260, 204 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the counter test, the region set-up and the choice between the second argument and the next sibling were traced).
  Folds the pending change in the viewable save-under count into the total (`deltaSaveUndersViewable` added to `numSaveUndersViewable`, delta reset to 0), builds an empty region with the screen entry +0x154, calls `miCheckSubSaveUnder` for the window's parent with the second argument or, when bit 25 of +0x7c is clear, the window's next sibling (+0x1c), and then destroys the region with +0x160; it returns what the check returned, and returns 0 at once when both counters are zero.
- `mfbGetImage` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x159fbb0, 292 bytes): **established.**
  Reads back a one-bit-deep image. The row pitch is the width rounded up to 32 bits in bytes (`((w + 31) >> 5) * 4`); when bit 0 of the plane mask is clear it just zeroes pitch times height bytes of the destination with `bzero`; otherwise it wraps the destination in a scratch pixmap header, makes a one-rectangle region of the request offset by the drawable's origin (+8, +0xa) and copies with `mfbDoBitblt` using raster op 3, then frees the region and header.
- `miRegionCreate` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15c9160, 304 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end; it allocates the record under the must-have-memory flag and never tests that allocation, and falls back to the shared empty block when the box allocation fails).
  Allocates a 0xc-byte region record with `Xalloc` under the `Must_have_memory` flag. When a box is passed its four halfwords are copied in and the data pointer at +8 is zeroed (a one-rectangle region); with no box the record takes the module's empty extents (four halfwords at +0x2860 of the data block) and the shared `miEmptyData` block, and a size hint above 1 allocates (falling back to `miEmptyData` when that allocation fails) a rectangle block of hint times 8 plus 8 bytes with its size word set to the hint and its count zeroed.
- `miRegionInit` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x17119f0, 232 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end, including the fallback after a failed block allocation).
  The in-place form of the same constructor: it fills a caller-supplied region record instead of allocating one, from the box when given or from the empty extents with `miEmptyData`, with an optional rectangle block of hint times 8 plus 8 bytes for a hint above 1; a failed block allocation falls back to `miEmptyData`.
- `miRectAlloc` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1675930, 296 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the three allocation cases and the growth arithmetic at 0x1675964 to 0x1675984 were traced).
  Makes room for more rectangles in a region's block, with `Must_have_memory` raised. A region with no block at all gets a fresh one holding the region's own box as its first rectangle (count 1, size requested plus 1); a block of size zero is replaced by one of the requested size; a full block is grown with `Xrealloc` by the requested count, by the request when it is not 1, and when the request is exactly 1 by the current count (doubling the block) up to a count of 500 and by 250 above that (0x1675958 to 0x1675984); it returns 1.
- `incrRRMpriv` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15da9f0, 276 bytes): **established.**
  Gives every window from the argument up to the root a resource-manager record where it has none: each missing one is a zeroed 0x4c-byte `Xalloc` block stored in the window's private array; the screen's initialiser at +0x50 of the screen record is called for the window when installed, otherwise the record's halfword +2 is set to 0. The walk follows the parent link at +0x18 and stops at the root or when allocation fails.
- `appendRequestQueue..AF` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15f9120, 244 bytes): **established.**
  Queues a window for the resource manager's deferred work. If the record's flag bit 5 (0x20) is clear the window is appended to the screen's pending list (head at +0x8c, tail at +0x90, each record's next link at +0x38 and previous at +0x34) and the flag is set; the request bits of the second argument are ORed into the byte at +5. The first call also queues `rrmWorkProc` with `QueueWorkProc` and every call registers `rrmBlockHandler..OE` and `rrmWakeupHandler..PE` with `RegisterBlockAndWakeupHandlers`, setting the two module flags at 0x173f374 and 0x173f378.
- `rrmWorkProc` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16d9d10, 232 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the 50 ms test, the per-screen call loop and the stored time and flag were traced).
  The resource manager's deferred-work procedure. It reads `GetTimeInMillis`, returns 0 at once if under 0x32 ms passed since the time stored at 0x173f370, otherwise it calls entry +0x18 of each screen's resource-manager record in turn (the screens come from `screenInfo`, count at +0x44), ORs their results, stores the time and the OR into 0x173f370 and 0x173f374, and returns true when every screen reported done.
- `odsyBlockHandler..L` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x167cc50, 236 bytes): **established.**
  Before the server blocks, drains the pipeline if the board record's pending flag (halfword at +0x68) is set: it sends the three settle sequences (0x10443 with 250 and two 0x10046, 0x10019, then 0x10443 with 150 and four 0x10046, then 0x10443 with 250 and two 0x10046) through the FIFO pointer at word 0 of the record, clearing the region's flag at +0x1cb8 before each, and clears the pending flag.
- `odsyDetachXvc` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x161b7f0, 268 bytes): **established.**
  Detaches the video-control state of a screen: from the screen's video-control private record it walks the channel table (count at +4, array at +8, 0x1f8 bytes a channel) freeing the block at +0x144 of each channel that has one and clearing it, frees the record with `Xfree`, and clears the screen's slot in `XSGIvcScreens`.
- `SetDIDMode` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1690920, 264 bytes): **established.**
  Programs a window's display-identifier mode unless the identifier already holds it. With the identifier index at +0x14 of the window record valid, it skips the work when the identifier's entry is flagged programmed (bit 3 of +0xa8), has the window's class (+2 against +0xaa) and its two mask words, ANDed with the screen's masks at +0x10 and +0x14, equal the window's (+0x20, +0x24). Otherwise it calls the screen's mode procedure (+0x28 of the resource-manager record), sets bit 3 and stores the window's mask words in the entry's +0xa0 and +0xa4.
- `DelayValidateClip..EF` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1681bb0, 240 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the arguments of the `TimerSet` call and both failure exits were traced).
  Postpones a clip validation (arguments: window, a second word; result 1 when a timer was set, otherwise 0). When the window's GLX private (`glxWindowPrivateIndex`) exists and its word at +0x18 is set, it allocates an 8-byte request (window, second argument) and calls `TimerSet` with no old timer, no flags, 1 millisecond, the callback `DelayValidateClipCallback..DF` (0x16b0e90) and the request. On success it records the timer at +0x44 and the request at +0x48 of the window's resource-manager record and returns 1; when no timer could be made it frees the request with `Xfree` and returns 0. A failed allocation, a missing private or a zero word returns 0 without a timer.
- `nfbCreateHWColormap` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1670920, 256 bytes): **established.**
  Allocates the per-colour-map allocation bitmap, stored at +0x44 of the colour map. The size comes from the entry count in the visual (halfword +8 of the record at +0): for a DirectColor visual (class 5 at +4) it is ((count + 7) / 8 rounded up to words) times 0xc plus 8 bytes, otherwise (count + 31) / 8 plus 8. When `Xalloc` succeeds the first word is set to -1 and the rest is cleared with `bzero`.
- `odsyGrowBitmap` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x169fad0, 288 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the height test, the doubling loop and the row-copy passes were traced).
  Makes a short one-bit stipple taller by repetition: starting from the pixmap's height (+0xe), it returns 0 when that height is already above 16, otherwise it finds the first height above 16 reached by repeated doubling (the loop is unrolled twice), creates a depth-1 pixmap of the same width (+0xc) and that height through the screen's create-pixmap entry (+0xec), copies the original rows (pitch at +0x1c times old height) and then repeats the rows by `memmove` (returning the new pixmap, or 0 when it could not be created), doubling the copied block each pass until 16 rows are exceeded.
- `odsyInitDIDState` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1615f90, 308 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the clearing loop and the eight special entries were traced store by store).
  Resets the resource manager's identifier table at screen start (argument: screen; result 1). The table is in the screen's resource-manager record at +0x98, 0x28 entries of 0x24 bytes. For each of the first 32 entries it zeroes the words at entry +8 and +0xc and the halfword at +0x12 (the loop is unrolled to two entries a pass, 0x1615fe0 to 0x161602c, and the first entry's halfword is cleared at 0x1615fcc). For the last eight entries (record offsets +0x520 to +0x626) it sets the word at +8 to 0x3c00, the word at +0xc to 0 and the halfword at +0x12 to 1 (0x1616030 to 0x1616088). It then prints "odsyInitDIDState: numIDs mismatch! %d<->%d" (0x172bd50) with ErrorF when the record's identifier count (word 0) is not 0x28.

## Backing-store wrappers for text, glyph and stipple drawing

The `miBS*` drawing wrappers keep the backing-store layer in step with what is drawn into a window. Each takes the context's backing-store private record (found through a module-private index in the context's device-private array at +0x4c): word 0 is the context used for the backing pixmap, words 4 and 5 are the saved operation tables. A wrapper swaps the saved tables back into the context at +0x48 and +0x44, calls the saved routine for the window, calls the same entry of the backing context's table with the position moved by the window's backing-store origin (halfwords +4 and +6 of the record at window +0x74), then puts the wrapper's own tables (0x1738d48 and 0x1738d28) back. Only the table entry differs.

- `miBSPolyText8..BEB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1620cc0, 248 bytes): **established.**
  Wraps 8-bit polytext through entry +0x34 of the operation tables, and returns the width value the saved routine returns.
- `miBSPolyText16..CEB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x17233d0, 248 bytes): **established.**
  The 16-bit polytext wrapper, using entry +0x38 of both tables and returning the saved routine's value.
- `miBSImageText8..DEB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16ab9e0, 240 bytes): **established.**
  Image-text wrapper for 8-bit strings, with no result. It finds the context's private record through the module-private index (0x16aba14), puts the record's words 4 and 5 back into the context at +0x48 and +0x44 (0x16aba3c, 0x16aba44) and calls entry +0x3c of the restored operation table on the drawable (the `jalr` at 0x16aba5c). It then calls entry +0x3c of the backing context (record word 0) on the backing pixmap (word 0 of the drawable's backing-store record at +0x74), with the position moved by the halfwords at +4 and +6 of that record (0x16aba7c to 0x16aba98). Last it stores the context's current +0x48 into record word 4, installs the wrapper's table 0x1738d48 at +0x48 and puts the +0x44 value it held on entry back (0x16abab8 to 0x16abacc).
- `miBSImageText16..EEB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16e70c0, 240 bytes): **established.**
  Image-text wrapper for 16-bit strings through entry +0x40.
- `miBSImageGlyphBlt..FEB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16c1ef0, 248 bytes): **established.**
  Wraps the image-glyph entry +0x44, which takes seven arguments (drawable, context, position, count, glyph list, glyph base); both calls pass all seven.
- `miBSPolyGlyphBlt..GEB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1722df0, 248 bytes): **established.**
  Wraps the poly-glyph entry at +0x48 of the tables in the same way.
- `miBSPushPixels..HEB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15efd80, 304 bytes): **established.**
  Wraps push-pixels (entry +0x4c). The window is the third argument here, so the backing-store record comes from window +0x74 while the context is the first argument, and when bit 17 of the context word at +0x10 is set (a clip-by-children style flag) the position is first reduced by the window's own origin (+8, +0xa) before the backing-store origin is subtracted.
- `miBSDrawGuarantee..BFB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16209f0, 240 bytes): **established.**
  Records how long a context is guaranteed to draw to a window. When the window has a backing store it finds or creates the context's 0x18-byte backing-store private record (allocating, zeroing words 0 to 2, setting word 3 to 0x7fffff and saving the current operation tables in words 4 and 5 before installing the wrapper tables) and, while the record's word 1 is 0 or 1, stores the guarantee argument there.
- `miBSFree..MEB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15fe6a0, 220 bytes): **established.**
  Frees a window's backing store: it destroys the backing pixmap (screen entry +0xf0) when word 0 of the record is set, clears the record's flags, releases a tile pixmap held at +0x18 when the kind byte at +0x16 is 3, resets the kind bytes at +0x15 and +0x16 to 1 and 0, gives the window a new serial number from the shared counter (wrapping to 1 past 0x10000000), destroys the saved region (+0x160) at +8, frees the record and clears window +0x74.
- `miBSCheapValidateGC..KCB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15db150, 312 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the slot load and store, the 0x18-byte allocation and the table installs were traced to addresses).
  Validates a context for a drawable through the cheap backing-store layer. It reads the word in the context's private slot (module-private index at 0x173f728, array at +0x4c) and installs it as the function table at +0x44 (0x15db178); in the cheap state that word is the saved original table. For a drawable that is not a pixmap (byte 0 is not 1) but has a backing-store record (+0x74) it first allocates 0x18 bytes (0x15db1ac). When that succeeds it upgrades the context to the full wrapper: it zeroes record words 0 to 2, sets word 3 to 0x7fffff (as `miBSDrawGuarantee..BFB` does), saves the context's +0x48 and +0x44 values in words 4 and 5, installs the full wrapper's tables 0x1738d48 and 0x1738d28 and stores the record in the slot (0x15db214 to 0x15db260), then calls entry +0 of the table now at +0x44. Otherwise it calls entry +0 of the table it installed (0x15db1c8), writes the +0x44 value that the call left back into the private slot (0x15db1f4) and reinstalls the cheap wrapper's table 0x1738da0 at +0x44 (0x15db1f8).

## Video-control and external-sync routines

- `odsyQryExtSyncNm..HD` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15cded0, 304 bytes): **established.**
  Names a sync source. Argument 0 mallocs 0x16 bytes and copies in the string "Internal Option Board" (rodata 0x172dd10); argument 1 mallocs 0x15 bytes for "External Sync Source" (rodata 0x172dd50). A failed allocation prints "odsyQryExtSyncNm: Malloc failed (0)" or "(1)" with `ErrorF` and returns null; any other argument returns null. A successful call returns the allocated copy of the string (the copy is written with unaligned stores ending at 0x15cdfc4).
- `odsyResetProc` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1632c50, 224 bytes): **established.**
  Server-reset hook for the video-control state: for the screen's record (private index `vcScreenPrivateIndex`) it walks the channel table (count at +4, array at +8, 0x1f8 bytes a channel), frees each channel's block at +0x144 and clears it, then frees the record; the same walk as `odsyDetachXvc` without clearing the screen slot.
- `odsyGetSampleRate..AE` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16c2d40, 240 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end; the decompiler's branch flow around 0x173f32c was replaced by the branches of the listing).
  Asks the audio library for the sample rate and keeps the module's rate word current (argument: a pointer to the stored rate; no result). It calls `ALgetparams` (device 1, the two-word parameter block at 0x17384d8, count 2) where the first word is the parameter id (4 when the module loads) and the second receives the answer. A non-negative answer replaces the stored rate: when it equals the stored value the flag word at 0x173f32c is set to 0, otherwise the rate is stored and the flag becomes 1 (an answer of 0 stores the rate and leaves the flag as it was). An answer of -1 writes id 3, asks again with the same block and writes id 4 back before using the second answer, which is treated like the first (0x16c2df0 to 0x16c2e28). Any other negative answer, and a second negative answer, set the stored rate to 0 when it was not already 0 and leave the flag as it was.
- `odsyQueryBestChannelRectangle..HC` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16f6610, 284 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the output pointers, the clamp and the unclipped path were traced, with the stack arguments located).
  Reports where a channel's rectangle goes (arguments: screen, channel index, the requested x and y as floats, then four output pointers for x, y, width and height, the last two passed on the stack; no result). The video-control record (slot `vcScreenPrivateIndex`) keeps the channel count at +4 and a table of 0x1f8-byte channel records at +8. For the last channel, on a board whose record at the board's +0x5c has a nonzero word at +0x1d0, it clamps the requested x and y to 0 from below, then pulls each back so that position plus the record's width (+0x10) or height (+0xc) stays inside the screen's width and height (halfwords +8 and +0xa, converted to float), and returns that position with the record's width and height (0x16f664c to 0x16f66e0). In every other case it returns the record's own position (+4, +8) and its width and height (0x16f66ec to 0x16f6728).

## Query, option and resource-manager window hooks

- `odsyQueryBestSize` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15db290, 100 bytes): **established.**
  Answers the X best-size query with a 32-pixel limit. For the tile and stipple classes (1 and 2) both output halfwords are set to 0x20; for the cursor class (0) each output halfword is clamped down to 0x20 when it is larger (0x15db290 to 0x15db2e8); other classes are left untouched.
- `odsyCanLoadOptionHW..LB` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x163fd00, 108 bytes): **established.**
  Says whether an optional board feature can be loaded. Option 0 always answers 1; option 1 answers whether the halfword at +0x3a of the record at +0x5c of the board record equals 3 (the `xori`/`sltiu` pair at 0x163fd60 to 0x163fd68); any other option prints "odsyCanLoadOptionHW: unknown option 0x%x" with `ErrorF` and answers 1.
- `rrmDisposeClip` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x165ab40, 116 bytes): **established.**
  Detaches a render node from its clip in the kernel: it packs its last two arguments into a two-word block and issues ioctl 1004 (`RRM_UnbindRNFromClip`, 0x3ec) on the descriptor at +100 of the screen's private record (0x165ab5c); on -1 it prints "Failed to unbind rn from clip, rnid = %d,errno = %d" with the node id and `errno`.
- `rrmCopyWindow` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x169c080, 120 bytes): **established.**
  Wraps window copying. It temporarily replaces the screen's copy-window slot (+0xe0) with the resource manager's saved original (+0x78 of its screen record), calls that original for the window with the region argument, and restores the slot's previous contents.
- `rrmCreateWindow` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15c6470, 120 bytes): **established.**
  Wraps window creation: it clears the window's resource-manager private slot, installs the saved original create procedure (+0x70 of the resource manager's screen record) in the screen's slot at +0xb4, calls it, and reinstalls itself.
- `rrmWakeupHandler..PE` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15ba4a0, 260 bytes): **established.**
  Runs the resource manager's work from the wakeup path, at most every 50 ms (it returns at once when the milliseconds since the time kept at 0x173f370 are below 50, the `sltiu` at 0x15ba4d4): it calls entry +0x18 of each screen's record and ORs the results; when the OR is nonzero it stores the current time at 0x173f370 (0x15ba550), and when it is zero (every screen answered done) it unregisters `rrmBlockHandler..OE` and itself with `RemoveBlockAndWakeupHandlers` and clears the flag at 0x173f378.
- `rrmMapWindow` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x156eaa0, 480 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the no-record path, the ancestor walk, the identifier choice and the request-byte actions were traced).
  Wraps window mapping (argument: window; result what the saved original map procedure returns). A window without a record, or one whose record is not registered with the kernel (flag bit 0 clear), skips the identifier work: when it has no parent (the root) it first calls `rrmInitRootWindow..AG` (screen, window) to publish the board number, and in either case it still maps the window through the saved original procedure. Otherwise it calls `incrRRMpriv` for the window's parent, then walks up the ancestors adding 1 to each one's record count at +0x10, calls the screen record's procedure at +0x20 with (window, 0, 0), and reads the request byte at +5 of the window's record (before that call). It looks for an identifier with `FindFreeID` and, when none is free, uses `StealID` if the request byte is nonzero and `FindBestID` otherwise. It then installs the saved original map procedure (screen record +0x68) at screen +0xc4, calls it, reinstalls itself, and acts on the request byte: bit 1 calls `FindFreeCID` and `rrmValidateCID` when the screen record's word +4 is nonzero and then calls procedure +0x1c with the window; bit 3 calls procedure +0x24 with (window, record, screen record).
- `rrmUnmapWindow` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x1586c80, 808 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: both unlink blocks were followed store by store, as were the ancestor loop and the saved-procedure swap).
  Wraps window unmapping. For a registered window it runs the record procedure +0x20, then releases the window's clip identifier by unlinking it from the identifier entry's window list (updating the entry's use count and the flag bits of +0xa8, or, when the window's identifier equals the screen record's word 0 (the identifier count, the value one past the last real entry), only setting the identifier to -1 and skipping the unlink), does the same for its clip-region slot (a 0x1c-byte entry of the table at +0x94 selected by the halfword at +0x18 of the record, with its own list links at +0x3c and +0x40), decrements the mapped-child counts of all ancestors, and finally calls the saved original unmap procedure (+0x6c) through the screen's slot at +0xc8 before reinstalling itself.
- `rrmProcessRequestQueue` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x15e7d60, 512 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the unlink, both window states and the return test were traced).
  Takes the first window off the screen's pending list (head at +0x8c, tail at +0x90, record links +0x34 and +0x38; it clears the queued flag 0x20 and the links) and performs what was requested for it. A viewable window (bit 11 of +0x7c) gets an identifier from `FindFreeID` or, when none is free, `StealID`; request bit 3 then calls the screen procedure at +0x24, and request bit 1 finds and validates a clip identifier (`FindFreeCID`, `rrmValidateCID`, when the screen's word +4 is set) and calls procedure +0x1c. A window that is not viewable but has the record's flag bit 2 set only runs the two procedures. It returns true when the list is still nonempty afterwards.
- `rrmBlockHandler..OE` (`usr/lib/X11/dyDDX/odyssey.so`, 0x16e43a0, 664 bytes): **established** (listing read end to end: the two timeout branches and the division by 1000 as a multiplication by 0x83126e98 were traced).
  Keeps the server's select timeout short while the resource manager still has work. It reads `GetTimeInMillis`; when under 50 ms (0x32) have passed since 0x173f370 it lowers the timeout (seconds and microseconds, read through the pointer in the second argument) to the time that is left, or installs a fresh timeout record at +0x140 of the data block when none exists; otherwise it runs entry +0x18 of every screen's record, stores the time, and if any screen still has work it limits the timeout to 50 ms.

## Resource-manager window binding, validation and default colour-map routines
